# Claim registry — SAV (save game)

## Sack cell lifecycle

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-SACKENTRY-590 | **Sack registration has different existing-record and creation paths.** `005477b0` keys the cell from `Sack+0x10 -> Position+0x02`, tests Dynamic bit 0 before lookup, and returns 0 when that bit is set. An existing record is copied as 52 bytes; any nonzero payload+0x10 refuses, including the same Sack pointer. An empty +0x10 is set and the complete payload is copied back, returning 1 without calling recomputation or directly writing a plane. Missing-record creation zeroes 52 bytes, captures current Cost/Static into +0x00/+0x01 before Static is ORed with 0x20, then refetches, stores the Sack and calls `005456d0` before returning 1. The explicit failed-refetch branch returns 0 without a local rollback of preceding creation writes. Registration does not directly write Position. | **High** for complete local branches, operands and finite existing/missing/bit/identity controls. The refetch-failure edge is static-only under the consistent selected hash helpers. Allocation failure, malformed object pointers, external mutation and whole-runtime reachability remain **Unknown**. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0293](../experiments/EXP-0293-sack-cell-lifecycle/) |
| SAV-SACKREMOVE-591 | **Sack removal does not test whether the Sack slot is occupied or matches the supplied Sack.** `005479b0` uses `Sack+0x10 -> Position+0x02`; a missing hash record returns 0. A present record is copied, payload+0x10 is set to zero unconditionally, copied back, and `005456d0` runs before the deletion predicate. Thus zero, equal and different incoming slot pointers take the same local clear. Deletion tests exactly payload+0x04/+0x08/+0x0c/+0x10, byte+0x02 and byte+0x2c; nonzero +0x03/+0x2d..0x33 does not retain the record, and the six layer pointers are not independently tested. A retained record returns 1. Position and supplied Sack fields are not directly rewritten. | **High** for selected complete removal instructions and finite identity, missing, collision, each-retainer and residue controls. Count 0 with a nonzero layer is an intentionally inconsistent local predicate control, not a claimed valid original state. Transitive allocator effects and caller lifecycle are not inferred. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0293](../experiments/EXP-0293-sack-cell-lifecycle/) |
| SAV-SACKPLANES-592 | **Empty Sack-cell deletion restores Cost and Static but leaves Dynamic at the preceding recomputation result.** After `005456d0`, the deletion arm refetches the record, writes payload+0x00 to Cost and payload+0x01 to Static, unlinks the hash node, and conditionally ORs the preserved current Static bit 4 into Static and Dynamic. It does not copy the restored Static byte to Dynamic or clear Dynamic bit 5. With payload baseline Cost 11/Static 0x02 and incoming Static 0x12, finite normal-return execution ends with no node and planes 11/0x12/0x32. Nonzero layer+0x20 with count 0 ends 11/0x12/0x37; this distinguishes the actual Dynamic write-set from a full-plane restoration guess. The recompute body never reads Sack slot+0x10: its other occupant/layer/baseline inputs still determine its writes. Payload destructor `00550820(0)` calls the empty `00541270`; final hash allocation release remains an explicit normal-return cut. | **High** for direct stores, ordering, decoded recompute and finite before/after values. Whole-allocator preservation, original loaded scheduling and gameplay reachability of inconsistent layer/count controls remain **Unknown**. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0293](../experiments/EXP-0293-sack-cell-lifecycle/) |
| SAV-SACKCALLER-593 | **Sack lookup and caller effects are separate from the local cell transition.** `00547c60(Position)` first requires Static bit 5, then a matching hash node, and returns payload+0x10 or 0. `0050f5aa` uses this accessor before choosing existing-container merge versus Sack creation; failed `0050f715` returns before its gold addition/value recomputation. `0050f715` calls cell registration, then `00526ba0` on success or the Sack's deleting-destructor slot with argument 1 on failure. Two selected removal-caller slices, `004f3e88..004f3ec5` and `004d5993..004d59d9`, do not test the removal return before `00519960` and `004f4e3e`. These are caller dispatch facts, not proofs of complete transfer/destruction effects. | **High** for accessor/registration-caller instructions and finite dispatch controls; **Medium** for the bounded larger-caller slice interpretation. Container merge, actual destructor/collection effects, exceptional returns and complete callsite population remain **Unknown** in this experiment. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0293](../experiments/EXP-0293-sack-cell-lifecycle/) |

## Saved crossing local completion

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-CELLENTRY-582 | **An existing cell's trigger can be dispatched before its actor entry is refused; a newly created cell takes a different path.** For domain1/2, `00544ec0` reads an existing52-byte payload, checks nonzero operation other than26, and calls a caster helper before testing occupied slot+04 at `00545001`. A nonzero slot then calls `0054a200` and returns0 without a local occupant store or recompute. Domain3 tests+08 without that trigger arm. The miss path calls `005453e0`, which initializes52 bytes to zero then copies current cost/static baselines into+00/+01 and inserts the record; entry refetches it and stores the actor, without revisiting the existing-record trigger branch. The same creator's hit path only copies existing payload to scratch and returns, preserving saved baseline/tail/residue. This extends the retained field meanings of `SAV-CELLLOAD-111`, not a new layout. | **High** for named local ordering and finite new/existing/domain/occupied vectors. Caster effects and callback scratch purity remain **Unknown**; a synthetic mutating caster cut demonstrates why caller preservation is not whole-callee preservation. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0292](../experiments/EXP-0292-saved-crossing/) |
| SAV-CELLFAIL-583 | **A refused footprint entry has no local rollback of its completed prefix or its mover caches.** Before the per-cell loop, `00544d00` writes mover+72 and+82..85. In store order, +82 receives the cached low byte of Position cell-X getter `00544a10`, +83 the cached low byte of cell-Y getter `00544a20`, +84 the low byte of full-X getter `005449e0`, and +85 the low byte of full-Y getter `005449f0` (`SAV-TOKENPOS-074`: these last low bytes are Position+04/+05). The first two getter results are cached before call `0054a620`; the full-coordinate getters run after it. Word+72 receives that call's AX; the probe's `0x1357` return is only an explicit cut, not an original runtime value or calculation. All four getter bodies are accessor cuts in this probe; their source laws come from the cited prior claim. No atomic four-byte snapshot or callback purity is implied. Each successful `00544ec0` writes its own record. On the first false return, `00544e75..00544eaa` returns0 directly; no earlier-cell undo or cache restore occurs. A2x2 synthetic footprint with a conflict at each ordinal preserves exactly the earlier row-major actor-slot prefix. The old `TERR-FOOTPRINT-147` all-or-nothing headline is therefore withdrawn, while its loop geometry remains valid. | **High** for the complete local refusal path and five controlled footprint cases per image. Unexpanded notification/caster effects, reachability of a conflict in valid gameplay and later repair remain **Unknown**. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0292](../experiments/EXP-0292-saved-crossing/) |
| SAV-CELLLEAVE-584 | **The selected actor detach clears a nonzero domain slot without checking that its pointer equals the actor argument.** `00545230` refuses a missing node or zero selected slot; domain1/2 clears+04 and domain3 clears+08 after only a nonzero test. It copies the changed52 bytes back and recomputes. Deletion then requires all four occupant dwords+04/+08/+0c/+10, layer-count byte+02 and operation byte+2c to be zero; residue+03/+32/+33 and the remaining trigger-tail bytes do not retain a record. The deletion arm restores cost/static baselines, invokes removal, and preserves plane bit4. After recompute and any removal call, success writes, in order, mover+86 from byte[Position+00] at `0054538a`, +87 from byte[Position+01] at `0054539c`, +88 from byte[Position+04] at `005453ae`, and +89 from byte[Position+05] at `005453c0`. These are direct field loads, not the entry probe's accessor cuts; Position is reloaded from actor+10 before each load. An early refusal omits those stores. Own-pointer and distinct-pointer inputs follow the same selected clear path. | **High** for the exact local tests, stores and call order, with18 detach vectors per image. Node allocator/removal internals are controlled cuts; malformed identity consequences, later repair and complete callback behavior remain **Unknown**. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0292](../experiments/EXP-0292-saved-crossing/) |
| SAV-CROSSNEXT-585 | **Local cell refusal does not prevent centered completion or normalize the next selected SAVE.** In `00548c60`, a false detach ends the old-footprint loop but continues through `0054abb0`, the new Position stores and entry; the return from `00544d00` is not tested before the existing snap test. `005495f0` then tests fractions, not entry success: the known centered cleanup clears mover+aa/+ac/+a8/+80/+a6 and the dynamic route at actor+178, while the static route at+15c survives these direct instructions. Progress3's center test can clear order+09/action+54 despite refused entry. Under explicit returning cuts, the composed refusal vector retains the conflicting destination slot, centers the actor, empties its dynamic route and the isolated selected serializers emit those current values. The Unit prefix serializes static then dynamic lists before the180-byte mover; Token emits Position's12 bytes and the cell serializer emits current node key plus52 payload bytes. This is no rollback, atomic snapshot or original resave guarantee. | **High** for the named caller branches and finite normal-return instruction vectors. First actual post-load scheduling, whole SAVE chronology, operation26 relocation and unexpanded effects remain **Unknown**. Existing step arithmetic, key repair and ordinary route cleanup are reused, not newly inferred. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0292](../experiments/EXP-0292-saved-crossing/) |

## Dynamic Group initialization and selected first SAVE

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-GRPNEW-576 | **The selected Group/base constructors preserve incoming Group+1c but explicitly construct an empty embedded Group+20 list.** `0050f81b -> 00518c10 -> 00518c30 -> 004492a0` writes the base through +18, not +1c. `00523040` stores vtable `0059c430` at +20, zeros +24/+28/+2c/+30/+34 and writes grow-by10 at +38. Group construction supplies +3c and zeros +40/+44. Two byte-pattern sentinels and two valid mapped source-pointer sentinels survive at +1c; an incoming populated embedded-list header becomes empty without dereferencing its old head. The pointer sentinels are instrument tags, not field semantics. Both lawful images decode identically. | **High** for named normal-return constructor writes and controlled sentinel discrimination. | SAV-GRPALLOC-577 | **The selected existing-page small allocation path does not clear a newly returned Group payload.** `0050fa33` requests72 bytes through `00572824 -> 00554390 -> 005543b0 -> 00554400`. The image-initialized threshold is480; requests72/80/28 round to80/80/32 and enter `0055cbf0` with5/5/2 units. | SAV-GRPCMD-578 | **The selected ordinary-command prologue does not mint Group+1c or populate Group+20; its wrapper deletes at most the first rejected old Group in one invocation.** `0050fa33` exits the scan after a zero-return `00449800` deletion through `0050fab5 -> 0050fac4`, contrary to the former every-rejected-Group clause in `AI-CMD-033`. Group LOAD restores its list and literal saved +1c; controlled `[1234,abcd]`/`10203040` values survive the next selected store. ## Restored Group AI continuation

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-GRPDISPATCH-568 | **One Group dispatch selects its order once; an empty entry forces ff, while an empty exit does not.** The complete `00533ae0..00533cb7` reads Group count before AI+20, writes ff only at `00533af4`, and reads the byte at `00533afd`. Its six-entry table and separate 11/ff arms lead to the shared tail at `00533c66`; all other byte values also reach that tail. The cell argument for arms2/4/5 comes from AI word+0a. A returning arm that changes AI+20 does not cause a second selection in this invocation. The tail rereads current count/head; an arm that removes every member leaves its previous order until a later dispatch's entry test. Independently decoded EN/RU instructions give 512 zero/one-member byte-order vectors per root, including all256 values; controlled callbacks return normally and are not asserted pure in ROM1. | **High** for the complete local switch, table, single selection and fresh tail entry. Raw-image extraction and all local branch targets close the selected body, not transitive callees or original loaded chronology. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0290](../experiments/EXP-0290-restored-group-ai-continuation/) |
| SAV-GRPMUTATE-569 | **The caller's next-member rule is identity search in the current list, including Stand Ground, ff and withdrawal.** Order0's inline search and helper `0053eb80` search Group head-to-tail for the just-dispatched actor, then return its current successor; a missing actor or final node returns null. Order3, ff and the shared withdraw tail call that helper at `00533c06/00533c5b/00533ca5`. Sixteen controlled vectors per root show that removing the current actor terminates that stage, appending a later actor can extend it, changing the order does not reselect it, and the withdraw tail starts again from current membership. Its first successful predicate calls `0052f090`; otherwise its second successful predicate calls `0052f3e0`. Removing the current actor during a tail predicate still permits that actor's remaining selected tail calls before successor lookup. | **High** for these caller/helper programmes and explicit mutation-cut traces. Actual callback mutation, malformed/cyclic membership, deallocation safety and termination under arbitrary repeated mutation remain **Unknown**. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0290](../experiments/EXP-0290-restored-group-ai-continuation/) |
| SAV-GRPPATROL-570 | **The AI-owned patrol path and a member's active ring are separate ordered lists; the path-copy setter reverses the source.** In complete setter `005353e0..005355bb`, the source is Group+3c -> AI+4c and the destination is actor+158 -> order+90. The source is walked head-to-tail, but each value is inserted at the destination head (`005354b1..005354eb`). The setter chooses order+02 from that head at `00535526/0053552d`, then prepends the actor's own cell (`00535549..00535566`). Thus source A,B,C produces own,C,B,A with current waypoint C, preserving duplicate values. Empty source reaches the unguarded first-value read at `00535529`; the published mission-INI route's nonempty admission remains separate. This setter is not a direct call of the ordinary Group dispatcher. | **High** for complete setter data flow, pointer bases and controlled nonempty/duplicate/empty vectors. Stop, allocator and list primitives are explicit cuts; original invocation after LOAD and unexpanded mutation effects remain **Unknown**. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0290](../experiments/EXP-0290-restored-group-ai-continuation/) |
| SAV-PATROLCURSOR-571 | **The saved patrol cursor is a cell value, not a persisted node position.** Actor-order LOAD `005390c0` restores148 raw bytes, replaces order+90 with a fresh counted-word list, and leaves the raw word+02 cursor and dword+04 latch intact at this local boundary. Walker `0052d940` consumes a nonzero +04 by updating post+00 to the current cell and clearing +04, then calls guard. Only guard result order+08 equal0 or b reaches the patrol continuation. That continuation sets +04 to1 even when not yet arrived; on arrival it searches the active list for the first value equal to +02, takes its successor or wraps to head, and writes +02, move order+08 and destination+0a. Duplicate cells therefore use first-equal semantics. A missing current value or empty arrived list reaches `0052d9d8` with null; no fallback precedes that dereference. | **High** for the complete serializer/walker and eight discriminating cursor vectors per root. Controlled unmapped reads identify the local invalid-pointer edge, not an observed original crash or a claim that valid shipped setters produce that state. Guard effects and original acceptance remain **Unknown**. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0290](../experiments/EXP-0290-restored-group-ai-continuation/) |
| SAV-GRPSAVENEXT-572 | **The selected next-SAVE consumers read current ordered state, not a retained input payload or constructor defaults.** Group `00511938` serializes Group+20 word list, current80-byte AI block and current AI+4c word list, then current members and dwords+1c/+40/+44. The word-list writer emits count and walks nodes to null, writing each current16-bit value; it does not persist node identity or an iterator. AI store writes its current80 bytes before dereferencing its current+4c list. Actor-order store similarly writes148 bytes before its current+90 list. Controlled LOAD -> dispatch/patrol -> store vectors retain changed order/cursor values and newly allocated list pointers in the raw block while emitting ordered values separately. The direct Group dispatcher neither reads Group+20 list elements nor dereferences AI+4c; this does not make either list unused outside that selected body. Group+20 semantics, remaining unnamed AI fields, whole-linker mutation and first-loaded/next-SAVE runtime chronology stay Unknown. | **High** for the selected normal-return serializer sequence and finite controlled vectors. Archive, allocation, actor serialization and behavioral callbacks are explicit cuts; no atomic snapshot, safe authoring defaults, all-path producer set or original resave is established. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0290](../experiments/EXP-0290-restored-group-ai-continuation/) |

## Saved Group reconstruction

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-GRPLOAD-560 | **Group LOAD reconstructs membership before restoring its trailing fields.** Player group-list loader `005102f4` allocates/constructs a fresh 0x48-byte Group, calls `00511938`, then appends that Group to the collection after the serializer returns (`005103b3/005103de/005103ea`). Group first restores the embedded word list and the owned 80-byte AI block/list, clears its actor CObList, then reads each Unit reference and calls ordinary append `0050f950` (`005119e3/005119ef`), not generic list LOAD `00527ac0`. Append detaches an existing `actor+70` link, AddTails, stamps `actor+70 = Group`, then copies current `actor+14` to Group+44. After all actors, Group reads literal dword+1c and separately reads/remaps +40 and +44 through the current identity map. Both remappers return null on a missing key; neither validates membership or a target class in its selected body. Order is the established SAV-GRPORD-058 rule; the new result is restoration precedence and the separate continuation, not a new ordering discovery | **High** for complete named serializer/helper bodies and normal-return local precedence; malformed references, allocation failure, reused nonfresh lists and original acceptance remain **Unknown** | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0289](../experiments/EXP-0289-saved-group-rebuild/) |
| SAV-GRPOWNER-561 | **Enclosing Player, actor Token owner and saved Group owner are not one restored value.** Player registers its identity at `0051135f` before loading groups. Token registers its own key and resolves actor+14 at `00510f9b/00510fbc`; Group append can read that owner, but `00511a3b/00511a45` later replace Group+44 from its independent saved key. After all groups, the 32-byte Player block and Diary, Player remaps its hero reference, flattens group members into Player+20 and stamps every visited actor+14 with the enclosing Player (`005113f9/00511404`). That suffix does not recopy Group+44 or change actor+70. A mapped discordant Group owner can therefore differ from the corrected actor owner at this boundary; a missing Group key becomes null, without falling back to the enclosing Player. Nine bounded original-instruction vectors with explicit archive/MFC cuts discriminate append-wins, Group-tail-wins and Player-normalizes-everything models | **High** for the local stores/reads and order; vectors are controlled instruction execution, not original SAV acceptance or proof of arbitrary callee preservation | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0289](../experiments/EXP-0289-saved-group-rebuild/) |
| SAV-GRPIDENT-562 | **A saved Group+1c is consumed on normal resume without being reminted as an authored id.** Group LOAD copies it literally. The normal world-resume call `004d1430/004d143f` passes zero to `004e3591`; its group loop indexes each restored Group using +1c at `004e37ae/004e37b8/004e37c3` before testing that argument. Zero skips the owner+44-based initial-stance branch at `004e37c9`, not the index insertion. Authored map creation instead compares and writes Group+1c from the type-6 loader value (`004e2f55/004e2f58/004e3006`, already AI-GROUP-009). Constructor `0050f81b` and dynamic allocation wrapper `0050fa33` have no direct +1c store. This does not establish all dynamic first-save producers or harmlessness of an arbitrary saved value. File-local pointer keys and this raw group selector are different mechanisms | **High** for literal restore, selected resume branch/index and authored contrast; all-path dynamic initialization, collision consequences and first normal-tick chronology remain **Unknown** | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0289](../experiments/EXP-0289-saved-group-rebuild/) |
| SAV-GRPAI-563 | **Restored Group AI is neither constructor defaults nor an untouched 80-byte pointer-bearing blob.** `005391d0` conditionally deletes the constructor-created owned word list, reads 0x50 raw bytes into `*(Group+3c)`, allocates a fresh word list, replaces AI+4c, then restores that list (`00539241/00539259/0053929e/005392b3`). Thus saved AI+4c pointer bits are not used as the restored list identity. Group tick `00533ae0` reads current membership count; zero overwrites AI order byte+20 with ff before dispatch, while nonempty reads the restored/current order. Arm0 reads each actor+14, dispatches it, then searches the live Group list for that actor before taking the current successor. Rate helper `0054d1e0` reads actor+70 -> Group+3c -> AI byte+44, otherwise actor word+8c. The selected later link arm can set AI+48 through a resolved actor's Group (`004e419e/004e4206`); skipping initial stance does not prove the whole linker preserves AI. Group+40's gameplay meaning and the embedded Group+20 word-list meaning remain unclassified | **High** for wire/pointer replacement and named local consumers; transitive dispatch effects, complete AI-field meanings, exact loaded chronology and original observation remain **Unknown** | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0289](../experiments/EXP-0289-saved-group-rebuild/) |

## Equipment event order

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-EQUIPORDER-552 | **The six accepted equipment methods do not share one event order.** Armor attach completes same-slot item removal, stores its slot, adds both defensive blocks, refreshes positive weight, sets flags, then applies Effects. Armor removal refreshes negative weight, subtracts both blocks, clears its slot, sets flags, then removes Effects. Shield attach removes the old shield, conditionally actor-removes and reinserts a two-handed weapon, stores the new shield, adds blocks, refreshes weight, applies Effects, then sets flags. Shield removal refreshes negative weight, subtracts blocks, removes Effects, sets flags, then clears its slot. Weapon attach prepares its owned Spell before old-weapon removal; conditionally actor-removes/reinserts a shield; stores the new weapon, writes attack modifiers/active skill, calls actor derive, then writes conditional timing and range fields, refreshes weight, sets flags and applies Effects. Weapon removal removes Effects first, writes attack modifiers/active skill, derives, subtracts the currently reread `u8(Weapon+0x50)-1` from actor range byte `+0x12c` with byte wrapping (`0050e30b..0050e31f`), assigns timing bytes `+0x134=8` and `+0x135=4`, refreshes negative weight, sets flags, deletes/clears its nonnull owned Spell and finally clears actor weapon. Attach adds the same current-byte range delta after derive (`0050e161..0050e175`); neither range operation directly reloads a definition parameter. Intervening callback mutation is not excluded, so the cycle is not a guaranteed restoration. Same-slot eviction uses item `+3c`; opposite-hand eviction uses actor `+40`. | **High** for named local control-flow ordering on normal-return valid, nonaliased paths, including optional displacement arms; runtime occurrence, callback mutation and exceptional completion remain **Unknown** | ✔ promoted (range-restoration scope amended) | [EXP-0288](../experiments/EXP-0288-equipment-event-order/) |
| SAV-EQUIPEFFECT-553 | **Equipment Effect traversal is forward and incrementally read, not a pre-collected batch or reverse removal.** Both walkers use `00518210/00518260 -> 005182b0`: save the current node's next pointer into iterator state before returning its Effect pointer, call that Effect, then fetch the pending node. Twelve original-instruction walker controls distinguish empty, single, AB, BA and two callback-cut mutations: changing A's next after A was fetched still visits B,C; changing pending B's payload/link yields A,D. These injected cuts demonstrate iterator sensitivity, not actual Effect mutations. For Token-state0, each normal-return application/removal runs general `00501a22` with multiplier +1/-1; all 50 static kind entries reach actor `+50` at `00502833`, including kind41's empty local arm, before the next Effect. State8 apply and states12/17 apply/remove have different branches; arbitrary Effect subclass overrides and transitive callbacks are not covered. | **High** for bounded iterator instructions, twelve controlled traces and all50 normal-return kind paths; actual list mutation by callbacks, runtime reach and unlisted overrides remain **Unknown** | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0288](../experiments/EXP-0288-equipment-event-order/) |
| SAV-EQUIPCALL-554 | **Ordinary command22 adds container and load events but no unconditional final Human derive.** Carried source2 first takes the requested quantity through `0050ebf9`. Destination1 calls actor `+38` at `004d693b`, reinserts a nonnull same-slot return at the source index, then calls `004f36f7(0)` at `004d6963`. Equipment source1 calls actor `+40`; destination2 inserts at the requested destination index, then refreshes zero weight at `004d69a2`. `004f36f7` compares prior/refreshed signed load-capacity quotients and calls actor `+50` only when unequal. Raw vtables select Humanoid/Human wrappers `004f705b/004f70cf`, which add no trailing `+50/+54`; Unit wrappers add `+54`, whose Unit target only returns actor `+1c`. The Humanoid/Human `+54` target is the already-published Token-value computation, not equipment derive. Command notification follows the selected load refresh. | **High** for the named command arms, wrapper split and load predicate; full UI admission, quantity/split semantics outside published controls, notification side effects and runtime chronology remain **Unknown** | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0288](../experiments/EXP-0288-equipment-event-order/) |
| SAV-EQUIPOBS-555 | **Intermediate equipment states have named consumers, not an atomic final-state contract.** Armor/Shield negative-weight refresh precedes defensive subtraction; a changed quotient enters Humanoid derive while the old defensive modifier contribution has not yet been removed by that item body. Derive clears live defence then folds retained actor `+fe` through `004f54f8 -> 005233a0 -> 004fa4eb`. Weapon's explicit derive precedes its timing/range and per-item weight update on both attach/removal. At the local Effect-removal boundary Armor has cleared its slot, Shield has not, and Weapon has not yet done its direct removals or owned-Spell deletion. These are store-order facts, not a claim that every Effect reads those slots. Actual reads include state8 apply's current actor `+c6/+94` and general dispatch's current `+d8`; each state0 Effect can enter derive before the next. Earlier callees can mutate the same fields. Complete transitive aliases, nested reentry, faults/nonreturns, first-SAV chronology and runtime visible values remain Unknown. | **High** for the named store/call/read frontier; **Unknown** for a complete intervening write set or runtime snapshot. No callback-purity assumption | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0288](../experiments/EXP-0288-equipment-event-order/) |

Level 2 ledger. Index: [registry.md](registry.md) · spec: [`formats/sav/format.md`](../formats/sav/format.md). Legend: `✔ promoted` (also in the spec) · `● active` · `✖ retracted`. IDs are permanent.

> **The body is opened (EXP-0046, 2026-07-28) and it was never stored the way this ledger assumed.**
> `[0x14, u32@0x04)` is a word-oriented run/literal code; decoding it resolves `SAV-UNK-006`,
> corrects `SAV-PTR-003`'s "descriptor head", and puts EXP-0041's predicted block-plane records in
> the file. **The decoded stream is now walked end to end (EXP-0048, 2026-07-29):** the object
> framing closes in all four saves (393/393 tag words), every object is attributed, the map's own
> placements reproduce the population, and the stream tiles with ≤ 70 bytes unattributed per save.
> **No `rom.exe` routine was read in either round** (no Ghidra), so every row below is carried by
> corpus arithmetic and named excluded rivals, never by a listing. The corpus is still **four saves
> of one map** (`scn:10.alm`, 80×80, two play sessions) — which bounds every claim about *shape*
> and does not bound the identities that close on quantities the model is not given.

> **EXP-0077 read the writer, the reader and the codec (2026-08-01).** Every row below was
> published under the caveat above, "no `rom.exe` routine was read". Five now have been: the save writer
> `FUN_004cee8f`, the save reader `FUN_004cf20e`, the world `Serialize` `FUN_004d0cb7`, the
> compressor `FUN_00517510` and the decompressor `FUN_00517950`. The corpus model survives
> byte-for-byte — the decode of all four saves is **identical** under both framings — but three
> things it said about *where the fields live* are wrong, and one thing it could not see is now
> the headline: **the world half of a save is optional.** See `SAV-FRAME-021`…`SAV-TRAIL-026`.

| SAV-FRAME-021 | **The container is a 16-byte header and a self-describing compressed blob, not a 20-byte header and a body.** Read from the writer `FUN_004cee8f` and the reader `FUN_004cf20e`, in that order: the writer serializes into a `CMemFile`, **pads it to an even length** (`004cf03a`, `AND ECX,0x1` → `SetLength(len+1)`), compresses `len/2` **words** with `FUN_00517510`, then writes `"Asg&"`, a **zero** dword, `0x0BAD0002`, the compressed **byte length**, and the blob (`004cf0cf`…`004cf12d`). It then takes `GetPosition()` (`004cf14a`), optionally appends a `0x100`-byte region, **seeks back to offset 4 and patches that position in** (`004cf19d` `Seek(4,0)`, `004cf1a9` `Write(&pos,4)`). The reader mirrors it: magic, then a dword it **reads and discards** (`004cf310`), then the version, then `u32@0x0C` → `malloc` and `Read` of exactly that many bytes **from offset `0x10`** (`004cf37f`…`004cf399`). Consequences, each of which corrects a row above: the coded region is **`[0x10, u32@0x04)`**, not `[0x14, …)`; **`u32@0x0C` is the consumed field** (the allocation size and the read length) and `u32@0x04` is the one nothing reads; `u32@0x04 − u32@0x0C = 16` is an **identity**, not a measurement; and EXP-0046's header field `u32@0x10` is not a header field at all — it is the blob's own first dword, which the decompressor reads (`SAV-CODEC-022`). Also settled: `u32@0x08` **is** a version and **is** ordered — `CMP …,0xbad0002` / `JGE` at `004cf323`, whose failure arm reports the literal **"Outdated save file."** (`0x5c5de4`), against **"Invalid save file."** (`0x5c5dd0`) for a bad magic. Probe: all six framing checks pass 4/4, and the bytes the new framing emits are **byte-identical** to EXP-0046's | **High.** Both directions of the format are read at instruction level and they agree field for field; the corpus then reproduces every relation the pair predicts, 4/4, including the two the old framing had to measure. Amends `SAV-HDR-001` (header length, body start), `SAV-PTR-003` (which field has a consumer), `SAV-UNK-006`/`SAV-SIZE-008` (where the word count lives), `SAV-EXT-009` (the coded region's start) and `SAV-VER-002` (Medium → High that it is a version) | ● active | [EXP-0077](../experiments/EXP-0077-party-and-save/) |
| SAV-CODEC-022 | **The codec is read, and `SAV-PACK-007`'s four Unknown opcodes are settled.** `FUN_00517950(src, srcLen, &dst, &dstWords)`: `*dstWords = *(u32*)src`; `*dst = malloc(*dstWords * 2)`; `i = 4`; **`while (i < srcLen)`** dispatch on `src[i] & 0x80` to `FUN_005179f0` (run) or `FUN_00517a70` (literal), adding each helper's return to `i`. Run: `n = op & 0x7f`, emit the following word `n` times, **return 3**. Literal: `n = op` (no mask), copy `n` words from `op+1`, **return `2n+1`**. So the four values shipped saves never use are all legal and all harmless — `0x00` is a literal of no words that costs one byte, `0x7f` a literal of 127, `0x80` a run of zero that costs three, `0x81` a run of one. Confirmed too: **there is no end marker** — the loop is bounded by the *source* length and never by the output count. The compressor `FUN_00517510` is the mirror, and its rule is one compare: `word[i] == word[i+1]` → emit a run, else a literal (`00517558`…`00517566`). Two facts a consumer needs that the corpus could not show: the compressor's output buffer is **`malloc(srcWords * 2)`** — sized to the *input*, with no headroom, so a body the code cannot shrink overruns it; and the compressor writes the word count into the first dword of its own output, which is why the count survives a `.chr` file and a network packet that have no container header at all | **High.** The decode is transcribed instruction for instruction and then re-run against the corpus: 12 516 opcodes over four saves, same output as EXP-0046, blob consumed exactly, declared word count reproduced 4/4. The four opcodes' behaviour is read off the arms, not inferred from an absence. Amends `SAV-PACK-007` | ● active | [EXP-0077](../experiments/EXP-0077-party-and-save/) |
| SAV-SHAPE-023 | **A save is two halves and the second one is optional — this is the fact the four-save corpus could not contain.** The world `Serialize` `FUN_004d0cb7` writes the campaign half unconditionally, then tests `world+0x2c` (`004d0e67`, `CMP dword ptr [EAX + 0x2c],0x0` / `JZ`) and writes **one byte**: `1` followed by the whole world snapshot, or `0` and nothing (`004d0e6d` / `004d0ed5`, `ar << (BYTE)`). The load arm reads that byte back (`004d1086`, `FUN_005188c0`) and branches on it (`004d109d`, `JZ 0x004d149f`): non-zero → destroy the live actors, rebuild the world's three sub-objects, **re-open the map from the install by name** and read the snapshot, `world+0x2c = 1`; zero → `world+0x2c = 0` and no map at all. **So a mid-mission save and a between-mission save are different shapes, and one byte in the stream tells them apart** — nothing outside the file is needed. `world+0x2c` is set to 1 by `FUN_004d00e9` when a map finishes loading (`PARTY-SESSION-008`), which is what makes the distinction "is a mission in progress". The four shipped saves are all mid-mission and all carry the world half, so **the corpus exercises one shape of the two**; the discrimination here is the listing's, not the corpus's | **High** that the two shapes exist and that one byte selects them (the two arms are a `JZ` and its fall-through, they are exhaustive, and the reader's branch is the same test on the byte it just read) / **Medium** for the gloss "between-mission": that `world+0x2c == 0` means *no map loaded* rests on `FUN_004d00e9`'s single write of 1, and no save with the byte clear has been observed. Discriminator: one save taken from the town screen — one play session, not an experiment **The discriminator was played and the save exists (EXP-0086, `SAV-CITY-030`): `game0010.sav`, 3 544 bytes, mission number 0, one player, no block array, no cell-record table, no session block, no `Unit`/`Building`/`Sack` class record — the byte-0 arm, witnessed. The Medium gloss is discharged.** | ● active (witnessed) | [EXP-0077](../experiments/EXP-0077-party-and-save/) |
| SAV-ROSTER-024 | **The roster lives in the campaign half, and is therefore in *every* save.** `[0x00609544]->FUN_0051102f(ar)` — the player list, and through it every `Player`, every group and every actor (`PARTY-ROSTER-002`) — is called **before** the shape byte, in both arms (`004d0e3c` store, `004d105b` load). Two consequences a consumer must implement. | SAV-DOC-053 | **The whole of what a save contains, enumerated from the top-level document `Serialize` `FUN_004d0cb7`.** Store arm, in order and with each width taken from the primitive it calls: `u32 +0x04`, `u32 +0x00`, `CString +0x28` (the map name), then thirteen `u32` (`+0x11c +0x124 +0x128 +0x12c +0x130 +0x134 +0x138 +0x13c +0x148 +0x144 +0x140 +0x80 +0x84`); `FUN_0051102f` (one `u32`, then `FUN_00527d70`: `u32` count + that many `ar << CObject*`, the `Player` list); at `004d0e5f`, `CALL [vtable+0]` on `*(*(this+0x14)+0xc)`, the dead-actor list manager (`this+0x14` points to the world and `world+0xc` points to the manager). The record is therefore **4 bytes only when the dead-actor list is empty**. Next is the world-half byte (`SAV-SHAPE-023`); when it is 1, the `Building` list (`FUN_005102db`→`FUN_00527a10`), the `SpellEffect` list (`FUN_005114b6`→`FUN_00527f90`), the terrain record (`FUN_00544a60`, `SAV-TERRKEY-056`), 4374 raw session bytes (`FUN_00539310`, re-derived here from the same pushed lengths `SAV-SESS-031` sums), and the `Sack` list (`FUN_0051149d`→`FUN_00527e90`); then `u32 0xbadface1`, `u32 [0x00609b0c]` and `world+0x118 → FUN_0053e800(ar)`, which reads or writes exactly 400 raw bytes. If that logical endpoint is odd, the outer writer extends the memory stream by one unconsumed byte before word compression; no byte is added at an even endpoint. The 400 observed zeros are loaded state, not padding (`SAV-TRAIL-026`). The five top-level list serializers use the same shape: `IsStoring`, `GetHeadPosition`/`GetCount`, `u32` count, and `ar << CObject*` per node; load reads the count and `ar >> <class>` with the element class pushed as a descriptor. | SAV-FOG-061 | **A save does carry a per-cell record of what the human participant has seen, and it is in the uncompressed tail, not in the compressed body.** The embedded `&YA1` state store (`SAV-EMB-004`) holds a section `Fog` with two leaves: `FirstState` (kind 2, int32) and `Data` (kind 6, int32[]). `Data` is a **run-length encoding of tile-plane bit 15** over exactly `W × H` cells in the plane's own linear order `idx = col + row·W` (`TERR-EDGE-024`), the first run carrying the state in `FirstState` and the state flipping between runs. Transcribed from both arms. Store, `FUN_00478c40`: `00479343 MOV EDI,[EAX+0xc]` takes the tile plane, `00479346 IMUL ECX,[EAX+0x4]` makes the cell count `W·H`, `0047934a`/`00479351` take `tile[0] & 0x8000` and `00479366 CALL 0x004cccb0` writes it as `Fog/FirstState`; the loop compares `AND ECX,0x8000` against the running state (`00479382`, `00479389`), advances one `u16` per cell (`0047937f ADD EDI,0x2`) and appends each run length at `004793b7`, and `004793dc CALL 0x004cdbb0` writes the array as `Fog/Data`. Load, `FUN_00477c00`: `0047883a CALL 0x004ccc20` reads `FirstState` into EDI, `00478854 CALL 0x004cd240` reads the array, `00478870 OR word ptr [ESI],DI` sets the state into each tile word, `00478877 ADD ESI,0x2` advances a cell and `00478884 XOR EDI,0x8000` toggles between runs. The load arm only ORs, so it depends on the `.alm`-supplied plane carrying bit 15 clear, which `TERR-FOG-087`'s census measured over 72 shipped maps. Corpus, 15 preserved saves: `sum(runs)` is **6400** on every save of `10.alm` and **20736** on every save of `20.alm` — the exact extents of 12 and 13 shipped `scenario.res` maps, identical on both roots; `FirstState` is 0 on 14 of 14; run counts and set-cell counts ascend with play (1 run / 0 cells on both restart saves, 29/152 and 35/248 on two saves minutes apart in one session, 273/3475 on the longest). The between-mission save carries no `Fog` section, which is the store arm's own `00479316 TEST EBX,EBX` / `00479318 JZ` world-half guard. Refutes `SAV-SEEN-055` and the persistence clause of `TERR-FOG-087`; `SAV-DOC-053`'s enumeration of `FUN_004d0cb7` is untouched and correct — it bounds that routine, and the file has a second producer | **High** (the encoding is read instruction by instruction on both arms, and the decode reproduces a quantity it is not given, the map's exact cell count, on 14 of 14 saves across two extents; the polarity is a field in the file, not an assumption; the index-list and bitmap rivals are excluded by the exact sums and by the varying element count) / **Medium** that this is the only tile bit a save restores: only the `Fog` section was traced into the plane / **Unknown** what the original **draws** from the restored bit — the render gate `TERR-TILE-079` describes ORs four neighbouring corner words, so the drawn extent may exceed the set cells | ● active | [EXP-0150](../experiments/EXP-0150-save-fog-record/) |

The store itself is **28 records / 19 leaves** on all 13 mid-mission saves and **22 / 15** on the between-mission save: `Character/Name`, `CurrentState/InBattle`, `Fog/FirstState`, `Fog/Data`, `GameOptions/{Wimpy,ShowHP,FlyingHP,Formation,Speed,ShowTimeFlow}`, `Inventory/IsOpen`, `Objects/Selection`, `Projectiles/{FreeIndex,IDs}`, `SpellBook/{IsOpen,Pressed,Shortcuts}`, `View/{X,Y}`. The two sections missing from the between-mission save are **`Fog` and `Projectiles`**, which is consistent with the store arm's world-half guard at `00479316`. | SAV-HEROID-065 | **Party progress is attached to each serialized `Human` object, not to its group, list position, or primary status.** The complete path is document `Player` list → `Player::Serialize` → group actor `CObject*` list → `Humanoid::Serialize` → `Unit::Serialize`. Neither arm writes Position first or reads packed cell `+0x02/+0x03`, unmanaged `+0x06/+0x07` or terrain `+0x08`. | SAV-600 | **Three of the campaign record's own methods read or write `+0x128` outside the SAV writer/reader: its constructor, its reset, and a floating-point reader.** Intersecting the 28 `disp:548` owners against the record-relative owners by function name — the method this row originally used to claim a universal negative — cannot see a method that receives the record pointer directly as `this`: such a method never computes `app+0x548`, so it cannot appear in that intersection by construction. It multiplies that value by a ratio of the sibling field `+0x124` and an external, session-scoped object's own `+0x108` when `+0x124` is non-zero, or by a fixed constant times that same external field when `+0x124` is zero — in neither arm is `+0x128` a divisor, so `0` is an ordinary multiplicand, not an excluded or special input, and every value in the corpus range `{0,2,3,5,6,9,22,26,27,30,33}` is arithmetically consistent with having passed through this reader. | SAV-608 | **No accessible save's Player-owned actor graph contains an individually addressable hired mercenary; every actor is drawn from one of nine fixed identities, none resembling the tavern's 1–15 type space.** `tools/savunit -mode party` walks every actor reachable from a valid human Player's group subtree across all 55 files (152 actors total; 82 over the 31 distinct SHA-256 byte streams, the same nine identities) and finds exactly nine distinct `(classKey, name)` pairs: `26 Danath` (45), `27 Naira` (4), `28 Fergard` (6), `29 Reniesta` (16), `42 Brian` (7), `54 –` (9), `58 –` (42), `200 Witch` (9), `201 Sarindar` (14). `classKey 42`/Brian's seven occurrences (four distinct save states) corroborate `SAV-CAMPAIGN-087`/`088`'s single-file `game0020.sav` finding ("the save's Brian object is in the Player group graph, not this campaign record") across the full corpus, not one fixture. ## Unit mover, route lists and order list

| SAV-630 | **The u16 elements of Unit's two embedded route lists (`+0x15c` static, `+0x178` dynamic) are `MOVE-ROUTE-004`'s own node `+0x8` packed cell — not an object or waypoint key — proven on both the save and the load arm, and the corpus is dominated by empty lists.** `u16list::Serialize` `FUN_00523100`'s save arm walks the live node chain from `this+0x4` and pushes each node's own `+0x8` before calling the element writer (`0052314b MOV ECX,[EBP-0x4]; 0052314e ADD ECX,0x8; 00523151 PUSH ECX; 00523156 CALL 005231d0`); its load arm reads the same two-byte element back into a stack local (`00523184 CALL 005231d0; 00523189 MOV CX,[EBP-0xc]`) and passes it to the append wrapper `FUN_0051c4b0`, which stores it into the freshly linked node's own `+0x8` (`0051c4d0 MOV AX,[EBP+0x8]; 0051c4d4 MOV [EDX+0x8],AX`). Both arms therefore agree the archived quantity is node `+0x8` — the same 12-byte node `MOVE-ROUTE-004` already derived from the two route-extraction routines and names as the packed cell `(y<<8)\|x`. `tools/savdoc -mode mover-route` reads exactly this field (already inside `unit()`'s own bounded extents; no new byte range is walked) across the full 3,448-record corpus spanning 16 save directories (`evidence/input-manifest.json`): the static list is nonempty on 518 records (15.0%, a range of 1..13 elements — every count 1-11 and 13 occurs in the corpus, 12 does not), the dynamic list on 177 (5.1%, 1..5 elements), and every one of the 2,371 decoded elements is a plausible in-bounds cell (x ∈ [10, 110], y ∈ [12, 132], `evidence/mover-route-summary.tsv`'s `element_count`/`element_x_min`/`element_x_max`/`element_y_min`/`element_y_max` rows). A second, independent corpus check corroborates the element-identity reading without replacing it: of 1,556 consecutive element pairs across both embedded lists (1,146 static, 410 dynamic), every one is Chebyshev-distance exactly 1 — an adjacent map cell — and the unit's own saved cell, decoded from the unrelated Position object (`SAV-TOKENPOS-074`, not from either list), sits within 2 cells of the static list's first element on 511/518 records and within 1 cell of the dynamic list's first element on 173/177 (`evidence/mover-route-summary.tsv`'s `static_pair_chebyshev1`, `dynamic_pair_chebyshev1`, `own_within2_of_static_first` and `own_within1_of_dynamic_first` rows). An object key, waypoint index or table key would not walk adjacent cells or anchor on a position decoded from a different structure | **High** for element identity: both arms are disassembled to the exact byte, the save side reached from the object it serializes and the load side from the object it constructs, not inferred from a corpus stride. The live alternative `SAV-EMBED-039` left open — that an element indexes an object or a waypoint key rather than a raw cell — is excluded because the node read from and written to is the same node `MOVE-ROUTE-004` independently derived as a route-search artifact, and node `+0x8` there is a 16-bit field, not a pointer. The corpus adjacency/anchoring check above corroborates this from a second, independent angle but is not the basis for the High: it does not by itself exclude every alternative that could coincidentally produce adjacent-looking values, only the instruction-level reading does. **Medium** for the corpus population reading: single-snapshot counts over the preserved-save population, not a controlled trace of a route in progress | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0311](../experiments/EXP-0311-unit-mover-route/) |
| SAV-631 | **Outside `Unit::Serialize`, six functions in the AI/movement module read or write the two embedded route lists: the two already-published extraction routines, three previously-uncharacterized runtime drivers that seed, consume and abort them, and one large uncharacterized search function that only takes the static list's address.** A whole-image `-disp 15c`/`-disp 178` sweep returns 87 and 33 raw hits; all but a handful sit in code (`0x401000`-`0x4ed000`) with no relation to Unit, AI or movement and are read here as unrelated classes reusing the same small offset — including the one hit between the earlier `0x4ec000` figure and the module boundary, `004ec794` in `FUN_004ec743` (`MOV EDX,[ECX+0x15c]`), disposed of rather than folded silently into a wider range: it is a getter that returns `this+0x15c` and `this+0x160` verbatim through two out-pointers (`004ec78e..004ec7a8`), reached after first calling `FUN_004eb2e6` on the same `this` (`004ec74a..004ec74d`); `FUN_004eb2e6` opens with the identical `CMP [this+0x15c],0x0` null-check that opens `FUN_004eb50f` below, the function this row already reads as treating `+0x15c` as a `malloc(0x100)` buffer pointer and `+0x160` as its element count, not a `Unit` — so `FUN_004ec743` is a further method of that same non-`Unit` class, not a seventh list accessor. Exactly six owning functions in `0x4f0000`-`0x550000` touch either field: the two route-extraction routines `MOVE-ROUTE-004`/`MOVE-PLANE-005` already publish (`FUN_005433a0` static, `FUN_005436b0` dynamic); `FUN_00544080` (captured with a preceding step-cost helper `FUN_00543e60` in one listing), which on an empty dynamic list seeds one synthetic node from the mover's own cached tail cell `mover+0x06` through the class allocator `FUN_0051c590` `SAV-632` characterizes (`005440fe..0054411e`), and on the ordinary path pops the dynamic list's own tail node, returns it to the free list and decrements count (`005441fb..00544224`, the mirror image of `0051c590`'s insert), calling the full teardown `FUN_0051c510` when count reaches zero (`00544229`), then walks the static list from its own tail to find a matching cell and hands off to `FUN_0054f900` (`0054426a`, not read in this experiment); `FUN_005492a0`, which on one arm defers directly to the reservation consumer `FUN_005495f0` and on another fully clears the static list — all five of its own fields including the pool block, again through `FUN_00570bc6` — guarded by a mover-status test at `mover+0x78`/`+0x7c`/`+0x9` (`0054932c..0054935f`), and separately seeds the dynamic list the same way as `FUN_00544080` (`005493d7..00549414`); and `FUN_00545990`, a near-twin of the same seed-on-empty snippet (`00545a01..00545a2a`) plus a bounded five-node walk of the static list's own tail chain and a branch into `FUN_00544300` when the dynamic list is empty. A seventh site, `FUN_00541e80`, computes the static list's address at `00542fc6` (`LEA ECX,[EAX+0x15c]`) but is otherwise unread here — at over 4KB before that instruction it is almost certainly the search driver itself, not a list accessor, and is named only because the sweep found it, not because its body was traced | **High** for every cited instruction (disassembled in full, `evidence/raw-listings.txt`, from the two whole-image sweeps in `evidence/disp-sweeps.txt`) and for the six-function count being exhaustive over the `0x4f0000`-`0x550000` module: every owning function the sweep returned there was read. **Medium**, scope-limited, for completeness beyond that module and beyond `FUN_00541e80`'s own body: an address computed in two steps, carried in a field, or reached only from inside that function's own ~4KB body is invisible to this instrument, the same blind spot `MOVE-PLANE-005` already recorded for its own writer enumeration | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0311](../experiments/EXP-0311-unit-mover-route/) |
| SAV-632 | **The `u16list` class's own append — used identically by all three sites, not only inside `Serialize` — increments count and only grows its node pool from a fixed chunk of 10 when the free list is empty; a full teardown walks any remaining chain, releases the pool block and zeroes all five bookkeeping fields, closing `SAV-WLIST-040`'s own open runtime mechanics.** `FUN_0051c590(this, next, value-via-caller)`: when `this+0x10` (free-list head) is nonzero it unlinks and reuses one node (`0051c61e..0051c62c`); when it is zero it grows the pool by exactly `this+0x18` (constructed as the literal `0xa`=10, `SAV-EMBED-039`/`SAV-WLIST-040`) nodes of 12 bytes through the block allocator `FUN_00570ba6`, then walks the fresh block backward linking each new node onto the free list (`0051c5e7..0051c613`) before taking the last one. Either way it links the new node's `+0x0`/`+0x4` pointers from its own two arguments, unconditionally increments `this+0xc` (count) by exactly one (`0051c640 MOV EAX,[EDX+0xc]; 0051c643 ADD EAX,0x1; 0051c649 MOV [ECX+0xc],EAX`) and calls `FUN_0051c6a0(1, node+0x8)` before returning the node — the append wrapper `FUN_0051c4b0` (`Unit::Serialize`'s own load arm) and the three runtime drivers `SAV-631` names all call this same routine directly, so the count and pool-growth behaviour is identical whichever caller inserts. `FUN_0051c510(this)` is the converse: it walks any node chain still reachable from `this+0x4`, calling `FUN_0051c670(1, node+0x8)` on each, then unconditionally zeroes `this+0xc`/`+0x10`/`+0x8`/`+0x4` and releases the pool block through `FUN_00570bc6(*(this+0x14))` — passing the value stored at `this+0x14`, not its address (`0051c56d MOV ECX,[EAX+0x14]`; contrast the allocator's `0051c5ae ADD EAX,0x14`, which computes and pushes the address itself) — before zeroing `this+0x14` too — a full list-and-pool release, not a plain counter reset. `FUN_00544080` (`SAV-631`) calls it exactly when its own tail-pop decrements count to zero, and `FUN_005492a0`'s clear arm inlines the same four zero-stores and `FUN_00570bc6` call directly rather than through `FUN_0051c510` | **High** — every instruction cited is disassembled in full in `evidence/raw-listings.txt`; the count increment/decrement and the pool growth/release are read from the routines themselves, not inferred from a corpus stride, extending `SAV-WLIST-040`'s own Serialize-only reading of this class to its complete runtime append/remove path | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0311](../experiments/EXP-0311-unit-mover-route/) |
| SAV-633 | **The third embedded list, `*(*(Unit+0x158)+0x90)`, is strictly bimodal in the preserved corpus — always exactly 0 or exactly 2 elements, and only ever one of two value pairs — consistent with `SAV-GRPPATROL-570`'s own already-published patrol-ring copy, though this experiment did not independently confirm that setter is the field's only writer.** `tools/savdoc -mode mover-route` decodes this field (already inside `unit()`'s own `wlist(*(*(+0x158)+0x90))` bound) for all 3,448 corpus records: 3,388 (98.3%) are empty and exactly 60 (1.7%) carry exactly 2 elements — no record anywhere in the corpus carries 1, 3 or any other count — and those 60 take only two distinct ordered pairs, 30 records each (`64,21;53,19` and `67,12;54,13`). `SAV-GRPPATROL-570` already publishes the writer for this field (`005353e0..005355bb`, `actor+0x158`\>`order+0x90`): it prepends the AI's own patrol path reversed, with the actor's own current cell prepended last — so a 2-element result is exactly what that setter produces from a 1-element AI-owned source path: own cell first, current waypoint (`order+0x02`) second, because `u16list::Serialize`'s save arm walks head-to-tail (`SAV-630`) and the head is whichever value the setter prepended most recently, the actor's own cell. A `-disp 90` sweep restricted to `0x530000`-`0x550000` (a whole-image sweep is unusably noisy on so common an offset) returns 36 hit instructions over 12 distinct owning functions (`evidence/disp-sweeps.txt`); only two are independently attributable to this field by name — `005353e0` itself and `Order::Serialize` `005390c0` (`SAV-EMBED-039`) — the other ten owners (`005301f0, 00530550, 005308a0, 005310e0, 00532bb0, 00532e60, 005336a0, 005356b0, 00537510, 00548f70`) are not distinguished from unrelated classes' own `+0x90` fields by this sweep and are left Unknown; the population searched is exactly this 36-hit, 12-owner set inside the named address range, not the whole image | **High** for the corpus shape: an exhaustive decode of the full preserved population, internally cross-checked (30+30=60=`order_count_2` exactly), and for the `SAV-GRPPATROL-570` citation matching the observed 2-element shape. **Medium** for completeness of the writer set: only 2 of 12 candidate owning functions in the swept range were positively identified; the other 10 remain Unknown, and this claim does not assert `SAV-GRPPATROL-570` is the field's only writer, only that it is consistent with the observed shape | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0311](../experiments/EXP-0311-unit-mover-route/) |
| SAV-634 | **On load, all three list surfaces are reconstructed as ordinary runtime state through the class's own append path, not through a separate recompute, and the module-scoped accessor census (`SAV-631`) finds no additional post-load consumer of the two Unit-embedded lists beyond the runtime drivers already named.** `Unit::Serialize`'s load arm and `Order::Serialize`'s load arm (`FUN_005390c0`, destroying and reallocating the prior list before refilling it, already published) both reach the same class append path `FUN_0051c4b0`/`FUN_0051c590` (`SAV-630`/`SAV-632`) that every runtime writer uses; nothing distinguishes a loaded node from one built by ordinary play. The complete owning-function set the whole-image `-disp 15c`/`-disp 178` sweep returns inside the `0x4f0000`-`0x550000` module (`SAV-631`) contains no function outside the two `Serialize` routines and the runtime drivers themselves — in particular no separate "first tick after load" consumer distinct from the ordinary step/seed/abort drivers a live unit would also call. This is a negative result bounded to that sweep and that module: an alias stored across the load boundary, or a consumer reached only through a virtual call this static sweep cannot see, is not excluded | **Medium** — the absence is read from the same two whole-image sweeps `SAV-631` already scopes, not from a runtime trace of an actual load-then-tick boundary; no dynamic instrumentation of the load path was run in this experiment | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0311](../experiments/EXP-0311-unit-mover-route/) |
| SAV-635 | **Four of `SAV-UNITFLD-049`'s five listed Unknowns — Unit-relative `+0x8e`, `+0x90`, `+0xa2` and `+0xa4` — are already named at High confidence by other ledgers that do not cross-reference it, closing them without new derivation.** `ITEM-LOAD-005`/`HERO-SIGHT-007` name `actor+0x8e` as the actor's own carried weight and `actor+0x90` as its derived load (own weight plus half the inventory container's running weight sum, `[actor+0x7c]+0x20`); `AI-SIGHT-092`/`HERO-SIGHT-007` name `actor+0xa4` as the `u16` sight radius in 1/256-cell units, whose high byte is `actor+0xa5`; `HERO-REGEN-021` names `actor[0xa2]` as the health-regeneration accumulator's own `% 100` remainder byte — a third naming, not the first: `formats/sav/format.md` already closed `+0xa2/+0xa3` before this experiment as the separately persisted regeneration remainder bytes (`SAV-REGENSTORE-529`, `SAV-REGENWIRE-532`), independently of both `HERO-REGEN-021` and `SAV-UNITFLD-049`. None of the three rows cited above cites `SAV-UNITFLD-049` back. For `+0x8e`, `+0x90` and `+0xa2`, its own dense enumeration names only the offset, grading each Unknown with no description; for `+0xa4` its text goes further — it already quotes the field's own value distribution via `UNIT-CTOR-004` ("sight `+0xa4 = 0` occurs in no record, the field taking exactly `1024 x {1, 1.25, 1.5, 2}` on `Unit`") — without resolving what the field means or citing `AI-SIGHT-092`/`HERO-SIGHT-007`'s own resolution. Either way, a reader relying on the framing "the unit fields `+0x8e`, `+0x90`, `+0xa4` and `+0x18` are promoted Unknown" would not find what any of them means without this cross-reference. The fifth listed Unknown, `+0x18`, is a different surface entirely — `SAV-636` | **High** — each cited field was independently disassembled to its own byte and graded High by its own experiment; this row only cross-references, and the four resolutions do not depend on each other or on any new reading performed here | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0311](../experiments/EXP-0311-unit-mover-route/) |
| SAV-636 | **`SAV-UNITFLD-049`'s fifth listed Unknown, Unit `+0x18`, is `Token+0x18` — the same byte as the un-adjusted first component `Unit::Serialize` begins with, already byte-mapped at High confidence by `SAV-TOKEN-034` — and no semantic reader, writer or consumer for it, or for its neighbour `Token+0x1c`, is named by any claim this repository publishes, this one included.** `SAV-TOKEN-034` itself states `Token::Serialize` `FUN_00510e5c` "was reached from … Unit's first instruction after the frame," i.e. before any pointer adjustment, so `Token`'s own `this` and `Unit`'s own `this` are the identical address and `Token`-relative `+0x18`/`+0x1c` name the same bytes as `Unit`-relative `+0x18`/`+0x1c`. `SAV-TOKEN-034` and `formats/sav/format.md`'s own Token table already fix the position and width — `u16` at stream bytes 23..24, `u32` at 25..28 — with no "what" cited for either row. `go run ./tools/claim -k "Token.{0,20}0x18"` and `-k "this\+0x1c"`, re-run in this experiment, return only `SAV-TOKEN-034`'s own byte-map and one false-positive substring match (`SAV-HUMAN-043`'s unrelated `this+0x1cc`); no row asserts what either field means or names a second reader. No whole-image census was run for either offset in this experiment: `+0x18`/`+0x1c` are too common a small-struct displacement for an unrestricted sweep to be useful, the same caution `SAV-633` applies to `+0x90`, and no code cluster analogous to the AI/movement module that scoped `SAV-631`'s and `SAV-633`'s sweeps is yet known to own the `Token` lifecycle specifically | **High** for the `Token+0x18` = `Unit+0x18` identity (`Token` is `Unit::Serialize`'s literal first-called component with no adjustment shown by the disassembly, not an inference from offset coincidence). **Unknown**, explicitly bounded, for what `Token+0x18`/`+0x1c` mean and who else reads or writes them: the search run here is the two `-k` queries above and the existing text of `SAV-TOKEN-034`/`SAV-TOKENLOAD-095`, not a fresh disassembly census of either offset. *Amended by [EXP-0312](../experiments/EXP-0312-tail-and-registers/): the "no claim, this one included, names a reader or writer" clause is retracted (`claims/retracted.md`) — `ITEM-VALUE-115` already named a `Token+0x1c` writer under `item.md`'s own "Item+1c" wording, which this row's `sav`-ledger-scoped `-k` search did not reach (`SAV-654`). The byte identity and byte-map stand* | ✔ promoted (amended) | [EXP-0311](../experiments/EXP-0311-unit-mover-route/) |

`SAV-637`–`SAV-645` are retired unused: the evidence supports seven claims, not sixteen, and the
brief's own range reservation is wider than what the two lists, the order list, the class
mechanics and the stat-block cross-references actually established.

## The stream tail, session registers and Token+0x18/+0x1c

| SAV-646 | **`world+0x118` is a heap-allocated, exclusively-owned 400-byte raw block with its own construction, use and teardown lifecycle — not an embedded array, not shared with any other pointer, and not itself the session object.** Its writer, `FUN_004cec1d` (world's own constructor; call-verified start, one direct caller `004762d8` in `004762a0` — the identical constructor/allocator pair `UNIT-GATE-012` already traces for a different field, `+0x84`, corroborating this claim's own identification of the function, not the source of it), allocates exactly `0x190` bytes through `operator new` (`004ced2e PUSH 0x190; 004ced33 CALL 00572824`), runs the block's own trivial constructor `FUN_0053e7d0` (`REP STOSD` of `0x64` dwords from a zeroed `EAX`, no field-specific initialization) when the allocation succeeds, and stores the result at `this+0x118` (`004ced6c MOV [EDX+0x118],EAX`) — the same instruction sequence `SAV-TRAIL-026`'s `world+0x118` pointer names. The generic (de)serializer `FUN_0053e800`, `SerializeRawBlock(this, 0x190, ar)`, dispatches only on `ar+0x14`'s storing/loading direction to the raw block primitives `0057b908`/`0057ba16` and exposes no field boundary of its own. At world teardown, `FUN_004d099a` frees the same pointer through a matching delete-style call (`004d0b9f CALL 005187b0`) and nulls `*(this+0x118)` (`004d0bb3`), in the same routine that also frees and nulls four sibling owned-singleton globals — `[0x5f21c4]` (session), `[0x5f22c8]`, `[0x609544]` and `[0x609558]` — with parallel construct/free/null lifecycles alongside `world`'s own `+0x118` member, confirming `world+0x118`'s block and the session object are separate allocations with separate lifetimes, not nested or aliased | **High** for the allocation, writer/reader chain and teardown free/null — every cited instruction is disassembled to the exact byte (`evidence/q1-world118-and-609b0c.txt`, `evidence/q2-session-slot93.txt`); the allocation size (`0x190`=400) and the one write site to `this+0x118` are read directly from the code, not inferred. **Medium** for "not shared with any other pointer": this experiment located the one write site to `this+0x118` and found no second store of the same returned pointer among the routines disassembled here, but ran no whole-image sweep for the allocated address itself — an alias reached only through code this experiment did not disassemble would not be found | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0312](../experiments/EXP-0312-tail-and-registers/) |
| SAV-647 | **Two of the 400-byte block's 100 dwords are debug-trace verbosity toggles, not free padding, save content or journal-like state: `+0x0` gates "turn tracing," `+0x4` gates "script tracing."** Two paired toggle sites (`0053cf70` for `+0x0`, `0053cebb` for `+0x4`) each read the current dword, branch on zero/nonzero, flip it, and log one of two paired confirmation strings read directly from `.data`, not `.rdata`: `+0x0`'s pair is a 24-byte "on" string at `0x5c89ec` (confirmed as this site's own other branch, `0053cfcb PUSH 0x5c89ec`; SHA-256 `ddaaf67eed84159d`…, cited by hash and length rather than verbatim) and `"Turn tracing turned off.\n"` at `0x5c8a08`; `+0x4`'s pair is `"Script tracing turned on.\n"` at `0x5c8a24` and `"Script tracing turned off.\n"` at `0x5c8a40`. Four further consumer sites gate a second pair of `Script:`-prefixed trace/error lines on the current value of the same two dwords before formatting them: `+0x0` at `005339e8` and `0053a11e`; `+0x4` at `00539af0` and `00539c4b`, the latter two guarding the literal strings `"Script: Trigger %d ( %d ifs, %d instants ).\n"` (`0x5c8720`) and `"Script: Bad inst..."` (`0x5c8750`). `-callers` finds 0 direct E8 callers for either toggle routine's own containing function; they are reached indirectly and no caller is asserted here. This answers the open "what is this block" question `SAV-TRAIL-026`/`SAV-DOC-053` left at "loaded state, not free padding": at least these two of its fields are ordinary engine debug-option flags with matching diagnostic output, which excludes a per-save journal or gameplay-state reading for these two specific dwords | **High** — the toggle/consumer instructions, the string addresses and their raw bytes are all in `evidence/q1-world118-and-609b0c.txt`; the paired on/off strings at the exact addresses each toggle site pushes leave no live alternative reading for these two fields specifically. The other 98 dwords are not characterized by this claim. *Amended by [EXP-0315](../experiments/EXP-0315-world-head-trailer/): the "`-callers` finds 0 direct E8 callers … no caller is asserted here" clause is retracted (`claims/retracted.md`) — the candidate's own `references.tsv` shows a direct `E8` call at `004d7441` into `0053cdc0`, the function containing both toggle sites, and `EXP-0312`'s own regen script never ran `-callers` for this heading. The toggle/consumer instructions and the paired strings stand* | ✔ promoted (amended) | [EXP-0312](../experiments/EXP-0312-tail-and-registers/) |
| SAV-648 | **`[0x00609b0c]` is a plain global int, not a struct member, with exactly three whole-image touch points: one zero-initializer, one unconditional save-arm write immediately after the stream's `0xbadface1` marker, and one marker-gated load-arm re-read.** A whole-image dword-xref scan (`-xrefs 609b0c:609b10`) finds exactly 3 hits in any section (`evidence/q1-609b0c-xrefs.txt`): `004d0eec`/`004d0ef5` inside the document programme's store arm (`FUN_004d0cb7`) — `MOV EAX,[0x609b0c]; PUSH EAX; CALL 005187e0`, writing the global's current value to the stream directly after the `0xbadface1` marker; `004d15d1` inside the load arm (`FUN_004d11d0`), reached only when the stream's stored dword equals `0xbadface1` (`004d15c7 CMP [EBP-0x10],0xbadface1; 004d15ce JNZ 004d15dd`), reading the stored value back into the global (`CALL 005188a0`); and `004d3060 MOV [0x609b0c],0x0` inside `FUN_004d303e`, the zero-initializer's first action, with exactly one direct E8 caller, `004d82cb`, inside `FUN_004d5e2a` (`evidence/q1-world118-and-609b0c.txt`). No other code in the image reads or writes this address by direct/absolute addressing | **High** for the three-touch-point enumeration — an image-wide xref scan for the literal dword, not a sample. A write reached only through register-indirect addressing, never resolving to a literal `0x609b0c` operand, would not be found by this instrument; none is asserted | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0312](../experiments/EXP-0312-tail-and-registers/) |
| SAV-649 | **Across 45 of the corpus's 46 distinct preserved save streams, the stream's 400-byte tail is zero in every save — extending the existing 4-save finding to that population with the same result — but `[0x00609b0c]`'s stored value is non-zero in exactly one, falsifying an "always zero" reading of that field and, from its neighbours, a monotonic-counter reading too.** The corpus manifest (`evidence/input-manifest.json`) lists 99 `.sav` files across 16 preserved-save directories spanning both EN/RU installs (byte-identical executables, `formats/sav/format.md:73`), 46 distinct by SHA-256; `tools/savdoc`'s own load gate requires the header magic `Asg&` and one file, `EXP-0261-owner-runs/game9000.sav`, carries `Bsg&` instead and is not parsed by the record-level tools (`SAV-DOC` catalogue; the gate's skip is now a diagnosed, named reason, not a silent drop — `evidence/census-summary.tsv`'s `manifest_unparsed_files`). Of the 45 parsed streams, 39 are original-written (produced by a lawful install without passing through this project's own writer) and 6 are written by this project's own SAV writer, classified from each save directory's own preserved `MANIFEST.md` or name for 15 of the corpus's 16 directories, split per file by name within the one further directory this corpus holds that mixes both, and overridden for one directory whose own name reads as an original acceptance run but whose one file is byte-identical to six files in unambiguous project-writer directories rather than to any observed original-engine resave shape (`evidence/q1-save-provenance.tsv`; the unparsed 46th stream is also project-written by its own directory's mixed-split filename convention, for 39/46 original and 7/46 project overall). `tools/savdoc -mode tail-census` (a new probe, `evidence/tail-census.tsv`) raw-scans every parseable save body for the `0xbadface1` marker, finds it exactly once in every one of the 45 parsed streams, and censuses the 400 bytes following the stored `[0x609b0c]` dword: 0 non-zero bytes in all 45 (`evidence/census-summary.tsv`'s `tail_census_nonzero_tail_bytes_saves`, `tail_census_saves_total`). The stored dword itself is 0 in 44 and `520` in exactly one, `EXP-0261-owner-runs/game0005.sav` — an original-written stream. That directory's own sequential saves read `0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 520, 0` across `game0000`–`game0006` (`evidence/tail-census.tsv`) — the value returns to 0 in the very next save after the 520, ruling out a monotonic or cumulative lifetime counter; the exact write that put 520 into the global before that save is not located (`SAV-648`'s three-touch-point census finds no fourth site), leaving how a non-zero value first enters the global an open question. | SAV-650 | **Session register slot 93 (`session+0xbea8`, the 94th of the 100 int32 slots at `session+0xbd34`) is written by literal, non-indexed displacement at three engine code sites, not two, and is not the only session slot the engine writes this way — slots 90, 91 and 92 are also written by a literal displacement (`SAV-658`).** A decode-anchored sweep of all 100 slot displacements (`cursorsurf -slotsweep bd34:100`, `evidence/q2-slot-sweep-100.txt`) — which, unlike a byte-pattern or `-disp` search, resolves every hit against the containing function's own linear decode and requires the decoder's own rendered operand to show the exact hex value before accepting a match — finds that among all 100 slots, only 90, 91, 92 and 93 have a literal (non-indexed, non-scaled) write anywhere in `.text`; slot 0 additionally shows the array's own base-address idiom (a `LEA`, two `ADD`, and 61 scaled-index accesses establishing or indexing the whole array, not a single-slot write) plus one hit near `004e4581` the sweep could not anchor to any decodable instruction start (the same unresolved case `SAV-651` previously described as "near `004e4215`"), and no other slot shows anything beyond zero hits, generic scaled-index access, a coincidental raw-byte match rejected by the decoder check, or a bare numeric-immediate match unrelated to array addressing. For slot 93 itself: the session sub-constructor `FUN_0052c0f0` REP-STOSD-zero-fills the whole 100-dword array, then explicitly stores four slots by literal `MOV` (`90=0` at `0052c1e8`, `91=0` at `0052c1ee`, `92=1` at `0052c1f4`, `93=0` at `0052c1fe`) — reached from `FUN_004d11d0` (`004d137d CALL FUN_0052c400`), which calls `FUN_0052c0f0` twice (`0052c53d`, `0052c6dd`). At runtime, slot 93 is then written by two further sites, both previously published but now precisely re-addressed: `FUN_005394e0` (`005394e3 INC [ESI+0xbea8]`, no direct `E8` caller located anywhere in `.text`), and an inlined twin at `0053375e`/`00533765` (`MOV ECX,[ESI+0xbea8]; INC ECX; MOV [ESI+0xbea8],ECX`) inside a function whose own start is `005336a0` — its prologue is the compiler's SEH idiom (`FS:MOV EAX,[0]`/`PUSH -1`/handler/`FS:MOV [0],ESP`), which `cursorsurf`'s automatic function map does not recognize and mis-anchors 6 bytes later, at `005336a6`; the true start was confirmed by hand from the SEH prologue shape and matches this claim's own prior wording. immediately after that, `CALL 00539900` (the pattern evaluator `TRIG-CMP-006` names) — `005394e9`/`005394f0` for the standalone routine, whose entire eight-instruction body is exactly this triple and nothing else, and `0053376d`/`00533774` for the inlined twin, which is followed instead by other work (`TRIG-EVAL-001`'s own player→group→unit walk). `FUN_005394e0` has zero direct `E8` callers and and `004d2595`, inside `FUN_004d2551`, gated on `server+0x4 mod 16 == 6` (the same signed-modulo-16 idiom, at a different comparand, that gates `SESS-TICK-004`'s already-published full-tick-counter increment at `mod 16 == 15` in the same function) | **High** for the population — all three slot-93 write sites and the 90/91/92/93-only result are read from a decode-anchored sweep of every one of The existing "`this+0x14` points to the world" sentence's own citation for its dead-actor-list access was wrong: `004d0e8f`/`004d0e95` (`MOV EDX,[EBP-0x90]; MOV EAX,[EDX+0x14]`) does not reach the dead-actor list — it continues to `004d0e98 MOV ECX,[EAX+0x4]`, one of four list-head fields on a wholly different object (below), not the dead-actor field. `this+0x14` itself is not `[0x005cd758]` and not the world: `004762b9 PUSH 0x174` sizes and `004762d8 CALL 004cec1d` constructs `[0x5cd758]`'s own `0x174`-byte object, and inside that constructor, `004cec55 MOV [EAX+0x14],0x6099d0` stores a literal address at `this+0x14` — not a pointer this constructor allocates (`evidence/q0-this14-object-6099d0.txt`). `0x6099d0` is itself a separate, statically constructed object with exactly three whole-image dword references (`cursorsurf -xrefs 6099d0:6099d4`): a constructor call at `004cebb1`/`004cebb6` (`CALL 004d1a85`), an `atexit`-registered destructor call at `004cebd2`/`004cebd7` (`CALL 004d1afd`), and the `004cec55` store itself. `FUN_004d1a85` zeroes exactly four dword fields at `+0x8`, `+0xc`, `+0x4`, `+0x0` (in that write order); the programme reads all four through the same `this+0x14` indirection at four distinct sites: `+0x0` at `004d0e81`/`004d0e84` (feeding a call to `005102db`), `+0x4` at `004d0e95`/`004d0e98` (the pair `formats/sav/format.md` and this claim's own prior wording miscited as the dead-actor access; it feeds a call to `005114b6`), `+0x8` at `004d0ec8`/`004d0ecb` (feeding a call to `0051149d`), and `+0xc` — the genuine dead-actor access — at `004d0e4b`/`004d0e4e` (`MOV EDX,[ECX+0x14]; MOV ECX,[EDX+0xc]`, followed by the vtable-slot-0 indirect call `004d0e5d`/`004d0e5f`). `formats/sav/format.md`'s own field names for the other three (Building `+0x0`, SpellEffect `+0x4`, Sack `+0x8`) are not independently re-derived here: this claim confirms the four-field structure, the read sites and the citation fix, not what each of `005102db`/`005114b6`/`0051149d` itself consumes.
