| SAV-658 | **Slots 90, 91 and 92 of the same session register array are also written by literal, non-indexed displacement — the same decode-anchored sweep that finds slot 93's three sites (`SAV-650`) finds these three, and no others, among the remaining 99 slots.** All three get the construction-time store `FUN_0052c0f0` makes alongside slot 93's own (`SAV-650`): `90=0` at `0052c1e8`, `91=0` at `0052c1ee`, `92=1` at `0052c1f4`, each a literal `MOV [ESI+off],reg-or-imm`. The sweep finds no further literal, scaled-index or immediate touch anywhere in `.text` for slots 91 or 92 beyond this one store; slot 90 gets two further runtime sites, both pairing its own increment with `session+0xbcb0`'s increment in the same block (`evidence/q2-session-slot93.txt`, `evidence/q2-slot-sweep-100.txt`): `00539cc1`/`00539ccf` (`MOV ECX,[EBP+0xbcb0]; MOV EAX,[EBP+0xbe9c]; INC ECX; INC EAX; MOV [EBP+0xbcb0],ECX; MOV [EBP+0xbe9c],EAX`), one case of `FUN_00539be0`'s own 34-way jump-table dispatch, reached from three direct `E8` callers (`004e2297` in `004e1f7f`, `00505022` in `00504ded`, `00539b68` in `00539900`, the pattern-evaluator `SAV-651` already names); and `0053bf7d`/`0053bf84` (`INC EAX; MOV [ECX+0xbcb0],EAX` immediately before, then `MOV EAX,[ECX+0xbe9c]; INC EAX; MOV [ECX+0xbe9c],EAX; RET 0x4`), in a handler block at `0053bf70` that is part of the same function as `0053bf10` (no separate prologue), with exactly one direct `E8` caller, `0053987d`, inside the check-evaluator `FUN_00539500`. So slot 90's two runtime writers are reached by two structurally different mechanisms — one from the same tick/pattern-dispatch cluster as slot 93's own unconditional site, one from the check-evaluator's own case dispatch — where slot 93's two runtime writers are both reached from the tick-advance cluster (`SAV-650`) | **High** for the population and the three write sites — the same decode-anchored, operand-verified sweep as `SAV-650`, covering all 100 slots. **Medium** for slots 91 and 92's own completeness beyond `.text` — the sweep does not see an indirect or vtable-dispatched write, so whether either slot is ever read or overwritten at runtime by some mechanism outside this experiment's decode is Unknown, not ruled out | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0312](../experiments/EXP-0312-tail-and-registers/) |

`SAV-659`–`SAV-661` are retired unused: the evidence supports thirteen claims, not sixteen, and the
brief's own range reservation is wider than what the stream tail, the session registers and
`Token+0x18`/`+0x1c` actually established.

## Player settings block, Diary arrays, and container tail

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-662 | **Across the full preserved corpus, `Player+0x30`'s 32-byte block is never anything but `AI-FORM-037`'s own formation byte at `+0x1f`; the other 31 bytes are zero in all 410 records.** `AI-FORM-037` already establishes the block's allocation, its constructor default (`FUN_0052ccb0` zeroes the block then writes `+0x1f=2`) and that `Player::Serialize` (`FUN_00511089`) hands it to `FUN_005392d0` as a raw `CArchive::Write` (`0057ba16`)/`::Read` (`0057b908`) call over a literal `0x20` bytes (`005392e3`/`005392f3`), gated only by `IsStoring` (`00449630`) — no per-field dispatch exists on either arm to discover. This experiment locates the exact call site inside `Player::Serialize`'s own body: the continuation both the store and load arms reach after `SAV-PLAYER-028`'s own seventeen-field run (`00511364..00511397`) calls an embedded object at `Player+0x24` (`FUN_005102f4`, outside this experiment's scope), then `CALL 005392d0` with `ECX` loaded from `[this+0x30]` (`0051137a..0051137d`), then dispatches `Diary` through a virtual call on `Player+0x40`'s own vtable slot 8 — confirming the brief's own citation and explaining why a direct-caller search for `Diary::Serialize` (`00511427`) finds zero E8 callers. `tools/savdoc -mode player-diary-container`'s `player-block.tsv` captures the block verbatim for all 410 preserved `Player` records across 45 distinct saves (`evidence/input-manifest.json`); `evidence/summary.tsv`'s `block32_byte_0x*` rows (`block32_bytes_ever_nonzero_count=1`) show byte `+0x1f` is the only one of 32 ever nonzero (408 records at value 2, 2 at value 0) and the remaining 31 are 0 in every record, with no exception. Brief Q1 also asks for the routines that write and read each of those 31 always-zero bytes outside `Serialize`; this experiment answers it only for the bytes the image names outright, and bounds the rest. The block constructor `FUN_0052ccb0` (`SAV-663`'s own cited allocation site) writes two individual bytes beyond `+0x1f`: `+0x8` and `+0x9` (`0052ccc4`/`0052ccc7`), redundant after the same routine's own `REP STOSD` zero-fill — the compiler pattern for a member initialized in its own right, not filler, so those two of the 31 bytes have a known writer and no known reader. `+0x1e` has three readers sharing the block's own `[this+0x30]` indirection through a second object (`0053cbb0`, `0053cbf0`, `0053cc20`, each `RET 8`, 16-byte aligned, NOP-padded, zero direct E8 callers): each treats the byte as a subscript into a stride-50 pairwise matrix (`base + 50*a + b`), so `+0x1e` has three known readers and no writer this experiment located. | SAV-671 | **The container's own `+0x1c` "insertion index" (`SAV-CITYSTORE-516`) defaults to a sentinel that forces every ordinary insert to append — `ITEM-CONT-004`'s own reading — and both of that claim's own non-default writers, the pick-up order and the equip arm, are exercised in the preserved corpus.** Constructor default `0x2710` (10000, `FUN_0050e7d3` `0050e7e5`, `ITEM-CONT-004`) is read by `Add`'s insertion helper (`FUN_0050e8e2`→`FUN_0050e902`, `ITEM-CONT-004`) as the target index; the generic insert primitive branches `index<count ? insert-before : AddTail`, so a sentinel default exceeding any realistic item count forces append on every ordinary insert — refining, not contradicting, `SAV-CITYSTORE-516`'s "insertion index" naming, and matching `ITEM-CONT-004`'s own "not a capacity — it is the index the next `Add` inserts at." A transfer helper `FUN_0050e837` copies `+0x1c` (and `+0x20`) verbatim between two container instances (`0050e8b6..0050e8b9`); a reset pair `FUN_0050f996` zeroes both fields (`0050f9cb`), both bounded within the sack/item module `0x50e000..0x511000` and both new to this experiment. `ITEM-CONT-004` already names two writers beyond the default: "both the pick-up order (`004d6791`) and the equip arm (`004d692c`) rewrite it before an add." This experiment's own ground-pickup write site is that same pick-up order (`004d6787..004d6791`, inside the actor command-dispatch function spanning approximately `0x4d6580..0x4d869c`), not a second, distinct writer: it writes `[actor+0x7c]+0x1c` (the actor's own container, not the source Sack's) from a sign-extended 16-bit value read off a command-associated record, then sets the same actor's own `+0x50=2` and exits to a shared dispatch point — mechanism `ITEM-CONT-004` did not publish. `ITEM-CONT-004`'s own equip arm is the resulting `Add()` call this experiment previously reported as unlocated: `004d6922..004d6959` reads the actor's own container at `[EDX+0x7c]` (`004d6929`), writes the insertion index at `+0x1c` (`004d692c`), then a few instructions later reaches `CALL 0050e902` (`004d6959`) through a reloaded `[ECX+0x7c]` — the same shape as the pick-up writer, closing that open item. Corpus (`evidence/summary.tsv`'s `container_tail_0x1c_*` rows, 3755 records): 3667 (97.7%) hold the sentinel; all 88 exceptions occur only on `Human`-class owners (0 on `Sack`, 0 on plain `Unit`), taking values 0, 2, 3, 5 or 8 | **High** for the sentinel-forces-append mechanism (`ITEM-CONT-004`'s own reading, independently confirmed) and the transfer/reset call sites (fully disassembled, new to this experiment) and for the corpus population (exhaustive). **Medium** for the ground-pickup writer's own downstream consumption: the write site is `ITEM-CONT-004`'s own pick-up order, and its `+0x50=2` latch and dispatch-exit mechanism are new, but what consumes `+0x50` afterward is still not traced | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0313](../experiments/EXP-0313-player-diary-container/) |

`SAV-673`–`SAV-677` are retired unused: the evidence supports eleven claims from this experiment's
`SAV-662`–`SAV-677` range, not sixteen. `Player+0x34` is not among them — `SAV-HERO-059` already
resolves it at High confidence (the field naming the participant's own starting character,
resolved through the identity map `SAV-669` also uses); this experiment's corpus re-confirms that
resolution without adding a new fact, so it is a citation, not a sixteenth row. `Player+0x44`'s own
meaning is likewise pre-resolved (`ALM-GRP-041`, `PAL-SHADE-012`, `ALM-PLAYER-069`); this
experiment's only addition there is the `Player+0x28`/`Player+0x2c` correlation folded into
`SAV-664`.

## Open, with the discriminator named

- **Whether a Building or Sack Position terrain key is used, replaced or discarded before the
  mandatory session-entry sender.** EXP-0249 proves that the Sack arm emits cell/sub-cell full X/Y,
  while the Building arm reads both and emits only cell X/Y; neither reads Position `+0x08`. It also
  finds the earlier optional ordinary tick whose computed actors and
  events prevent an absolute-first static classification (`SAV-POSTLOAD-220`, `221`). A safely
  witnessed cross-process load with the pre-entry tick controlled, followed by position use and a
  fresh save, discriminates raw-key survival from an earlier alias writer, repair or destructor.
  The city arm has no loaded Building/Sack; retained-actor replacement is conditional on the entry
  boolean, saved Player latch and seating state. Actor entry consumes only Items admitted by its
  recipient/type/ownership gates and eligible Effects. Sack pickup moves quantity-one Items into an
  existing actor container, can deep-copy Effects during a stack split and then deletes the source
  container (`SAV-POSTLOAD-222`, `223`); only their absolute ordering against the pre-entry tick
  remains open.
- **The remaining unnamed stored state:** members inside the 4374-byte session region past
  `SAV-SESS-031`, and `Group+0x4c`/`Group+0x20`'s own two `u16` list contents (unopened by this
  repository). The `Diary` arrays are closed by `SAV-667`–`SAV-669`: both are sized once at
  construction from one global catalog and resized again on every load from the stream's own
  count, `word[i] = 1024 - dword[i]` exactly across the corpus, and `Diary+0x2c` resolves to the
  Diary's own enclosing `Player` on all 410 Player-owned records (the other 132 are actor-owned
  and null); what writes the dword array's own nonzero values, and what the six per-unit-type
  counters those values populate actually count (`SAV-668`), are the sharper open items left
  there. The three Unit/order-relative
  `u16` lists' element identity, corpus shape and a bounded runtime-accessor census — their
  writers and consumers, once the next instrument — are closed by `SAV-630`–`SAV-634`. `SAV-633`'s own 10-of-12 unidentified `+0x90`
  owning functions are the sharper open item left in the same neighbourhood. `SAV-636`'s
  `Token+0x18`/`+0x1c` now have their serialization mechanics (`SAV-653`), one subclass's own
  published meaning for `+0x1c` (`Item`, `SAV-654`), a full corpus population for both fields
  (`SAV-655`, `SAV-656`), and, in the located emission paths, an operational recipient-mask
  meaning and clear-then-republish lifecycle for `+0x18` (`SAV-678`); what remains open is
  whether that meaning generalizes to every class, `+0x1c`'s meaning for every class but `Item`,
  and the five `+0x18` exceptions `SAV-655` could not explain.
- **The one unanchored slot-0 hit.** A decode-anchored sweep of all 100 slot displacements
  (`SAV-650`, `SAV-658`) now covers every slot, not only 93, and finds literal writes at exactly
  90, 91, 92 and 93 — closing the completeness question `SAV-651` originally left open. One hit
  remains genuinely unresolved: a raw match near `0x4e4581`, inside a routine the sweep's function
  map anchors to `0x4e4215`, could not be linearly decoded to a landing instruction in two
  attempts, and `0x4e4215`'s own first byte (`0x9a`, a far-call opcode this decoder's covered
  subset does not include) suggests that anchor may itself be a false function-start, not this
  decoder's only gap. What instruction is actually at `0x4e4581` is Unknown.
- **A save of a 256×256 map.** The window `[0x807, 0x807+0xe5e7)` was only ever exercised by an
  80×80 map, where nothing is past its high end. On a 256-wide plane rows 238..255 fall outside it
  entirely, so a save of `Islands`/`Tomb`/`scn:140` is the one measurement that tests the bound the
  other way — and it costs one play session, not an experiment. It would also test `SAV-ID-015`'s
  ordinal law on a second map.
- **Opcodes `0x00`, `0x7f`, `0x80`, `0x81`** (`SAV-PACK-007`): now a census — 0 occurrences over
  12 516 positions — but still only an absence. Settled by reading the codec in `rom.exe`.
- ~~**Whether a load re-runs the terrain ingest** (`SAV-BLOCK-012`)~~ — closed by `SAV-LOAD-057`:
  the caller builds the terrain from the `.alm`, ingest and all, before applying the records.

## Primitive encoding boundaries and nonminimal-reader domain

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-758 | **The primitive count and CString readers admit nonminimal successful-IO prefixes. Original `0057bc10` returns 1 from a u16 `0xffff`/u32=1 prefix; `0057b629` returns 1 from ff/u16=1 and from ff/u16 `0xffff`/u32=1, consuming all 6, 3, 7 bytes respectively. No shortest-form rejection occurs in these selected reader branches. The CString writer threshold is `0xfffe`, unlike generic count threshold `0xffff`: ff/`0xfffe` is a Unicode sentinel, not length 65534. Forty-seven assertions execute original branching instructions with only primitive archive IO cuts; existing SAV-WLIST-040 and SAV-FULLREAD-252 supply the broader grammar. No complete-SAV acceptance, allocation safety or normal writer population is implied.** | High for the bounded primitive instruction paths and boundary/nonminimal vectors; full reader/writer/runtime generality remains Unknown. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0319](../experiments/EXP-0319-sav-generality/), RESULT.md and evidence/closure/ |

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-726 | **Command `0x46` parameter 3 produces Player+0x58 through arm `004d7350` on the Player resolved by `004d4c6e`. For the signed dword at `cmd+0x0e`: `0..2` stores `(2-x)*50`; `3..100` stores `x`; any other value retains the old value. Both stores (`004d7385`, `004d73a8`) and the no-store range (`x < 0` or `x > 100`) all join at `004d73ab` and run the same actor walk unconditionally, dispatching each member's virtual `+0x50` derive at `004d740f`. The original selector table assigns parameter 1, not 3, to the withdraw calls `004d72ae`/`004d72d0`. `UNIT-DERIVE-003`'s unit derive `004f5946` and `HERO-MP-006`'s hero arm already publish that same virtual slot consuming Player+0x58 through `actor+0x14` as the percentage that scales `manaMax` into the AI's mana floor; this experiment adds the producer side of that field, not an independent typing of the consumer side. `SAV-666`'s bounded missed-writer surface is narrowed by this positive route, not closed: the five unattributed small-routine writes and any writer entirely outside the old searched interval are not excluded. Player serialization is still raw transport, not this command normalization. `SAV-666`'s own corpus values are two of this law's three outputs — selector 1's 50 and selector 2's 0 cover 98 of 410 records exactly, the constructor default 95 covers the remaining 312, and selector 0's 100 is absent from the corpus.** | High for receiver resolution, the selector mapping and the 33 instruction vectors, reproduced value-for-value under an independent emulator plus a wider synthetic sweep beyond the committed set. **Unknown**: whether any ordinary UI command emission reaches this arm; which classes other than the unit and human families reachable through `Player+0x20` change the `+0x50` derive target; whether selector 0's 100 output ever reaches a shipped save; and `Player+0x50` itself — a distinct residual field, not the derive slot — which this experiment did not locate. | ✔ promoted (branch candidate) | [EXP-0317](../experiments/EXP-0317-campaign-player-residuals/), RESULT.md and evidence/closure/ |

## Token recipient-mask semantics

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-678 | **Token+0x18 is a 16-bit recipient publication mask in the located emission paths, both set and cleared there, not a class discriminator.** Complete helpers 004f2885/004f28a8/004f28d0 respectively return the raw intersection with Player+0x2c, return 1 exactly when that intersection is zero, and OR the recipient's bits into Token+0x18; reset 004f2639 zeroes three fields, not one — word +0x18, dword +0x1c and dword +0x4 — on a construction whose vtable's own second slot is SAV-653's Token::Serialize. Five instruction sites in four functions clear one recipient's bit with the complementary idiom (004d1f00/004d1f1c in FUN_004d1e14; 004e9d06 in FUN_004e9c8c; 0050f7bc in FUN_0050f77e; 0050ffe7 in FUN_0050ff91); FUN_004e9c8c clears, then calls the existing sender on the same recipient, so the field is revoked and republished state, not a monotone accumulator, and a zero bit does not mean a recipient was never published. Two further sites zero the whole field (004cfcd7 in FUN_004cf8d8, paired with clearing the same +0x4c bit the sender itself tests; 004d8a4e in FUN_004d8963), and a second constructor of the same class (FUN_004f26fd, same vtable literal) copies +0x18 with the token's other fields rather than resetting it. The sender population is three functions plus one that inlines the same step: 004e7de3 (negative test, then setter, a dedup guard), 004e873b (the intersection test), and 004e935d (a third caller of the negative test, branching on a virtual dispatch to either 004e7de3 or 004e8eb3); 004e8eb3 implements the OR-publish step inline twice and ORs in 0xffff — every bit — when its recipient argument is null, the discriminator against a class flag or a Boolean. The three helpers have exactly four call sites image-wide (004e8681, 004e8702, 004e9377, 004e87ff: all CALL, zero JMP, zero absolute-dword references), a complete census, not a located one. The existing SAV-664 participant-mask producer and SAV-653 wire transport are reused. This does not establish a universal fog-of-war meaning, a class discriminator, every clearing/inline site image-wide, or the cause of every corpus exception.** | High for the three helper laws, the three-field reset, the clear-then-republish mechanism at the four named clear sites, and the complete four-site call census of the three helpers; Medium for the breadth of the sender/clearer population, since the third sender, the inline publisher and the clearing sites were found by reading the functions the helper census and existing references reached, not by an image-wide instruction-pattern sweep for every `and`/`or` on a 16-bit [x+0x18] operand — the closest such sweep (evidence/disp16-0x18.tsv, restricted to that exact 0x66-prefixed form) returns 54 anchored hits, 28 non-stack, each individually checked against Token's own object identity; Unknown for a universal fog-of-war meaning, a class discriminator and the cause of every corpus exception. | ✔ promoted (branch candidate) | [EXP-0314](../experiments/EXP-0314-token-group-residuals/), RESULT.md and evidence/closure/ |

## Item Effects-list class membership and Token+0x0c

| ID | Claim | Confidence | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SAV-774 | **`CArchive::ReadObject` (`0057d47c`) class-checks every typed reference it resolves — a class name's first occurrence as well as a repeat — and what a typed reference permits is any class *derived from* the one the caller requested, which for an item's own Effects list is exactly the two-class set `{Effect, Effect_DirectDamage}`.** Entry: `ESI=this`, `[EBP+0x8]=pClassRefRequested`. `0057d48e PUSH [EBP+0x8]` hands the requested class to `0057d6bc` **before** the branch at `0057d498`, so the check has already run by the time either of `ReadObject`'s own arms is entered. `0057d6bc` is `ReadClass(pClassRefRequested, pSchema, pObTag)` (`RET 0xc`; `*pSchema` written at `0057d84f`, `*pObTag` at `0057d85d`; `0057d6c9`..`0057d6df` rejects a requested class whose own `+0x8` schema word is `0xffff`), and the stream's own 16-bit tag selects one of three paths through it. No class-tag bit (`0057d727 TEST [EBP-0x18],EDI` with `EDI=0x80000000`): `*pObTag` is written and null returned (`0057d72c`..`0057d744`) — the object-back-reference case `ReadObject` then handles itself. `wTag == 0xffff` (`0057d749 CMP [EBP-0xe],BX`): first occurrence, the class name is read out of the stream by `0057d758 CALL 0057b6ce`, the schema is compared, then `0057d7e7 JMP 0057d82e`. Otherwise a class index resolves an already-seen class out of the archive's own load array (`0057d814 MOV EBX,[EAX+EDI*4]`) and falls into `0057d82e`. **Both class paths therefore reach `0057d82e`**: `CMP [EBP+0x8],0x0` / `0057d832 JZ 0057d84f` — the only branch that skips the check, and taken only when the caller requested no class — then `0057d834 PUSH [EBP+0x8]`, `0057d837 MOV ECX,EBX` (the class the stream named), `0057d839 CALL 005727e6`, and `0057d845 PUSH 0x6` / `CALL 0057d940` when that returns zero. `005727e6` is a derivation walk, not an identity test: it walks `ECX` up the descriptor `+0x10` base chain (`005727f0 MOV ECX,[ECX+0x10]`, `005727f3` back to the entry), returning 1 on any ancestor match and 0 when the chain reaches null. `ReadObject`'s own object-back-reference arm applies the identical walk to the already-constructed object (`0057d4cb CALL 0057272f` = `GetRuntimeClass` through vtable slot 0, then `005727e6`) and reports the same error code 6 (`0057d4d4`..`0057d4d9`). The only unchecked returns in either function are a null requested class and a null load-array entry (`0057d4be JZ 0057d522`). `Item::Serialize`'s own read of its Effects list (`0x51160e`/`0x511611 CALL 00527b70`) reaches `0057d47c` through wrapper `005010e9` (`0x5010ec PUSH 0x5c3160` / `0x5010f4 CALL 0057d47c`), which always requests a non-null class, so no reference in that list can take the null-request bypass. A byte-granular dword scan of every section of the image returns exactly one non-`.text` dword holding `0x5c3160` — `0x5c3188`, `Effect_DirectDamage`'s own `+0x10` base slot — and none holding `0x5c3178`, so the statically initialised derived-class closure of `Effect` (descriptor `0x5c3160`, name `0x5c58cc`, size `0x48`, base `0x5c2468` `Token`) is exactly `Effect` itself and `Effect_DirectDamage` (descriptor `0x5c3178`, name `0x5c58d4`, size `0x60`), consistent with `SAV-TOKENLOAD-093`'s already-published base-chain enumeration of the same descriptor set. Every other class name in that slot fails `005727e6` and raises archive error 6. | **High.** Both functions are read from entry to terminating `RET` (`0057d47c`..`0057d528`, `0057d6bc`..`0057d879`), so their control flow is enumerated, not sampled. The live alternative this excludes is the one this row previously asserted and `formats/sav/format.md` previously published — a reader that validates a typed reference only against an already-resolved back-reference and constructs a first occurrence by name alone — and it is excluded by the two transfers that converge on `0057d82e` before either class path can return (`0057d7e7 JMP` from the first-occurrence path, the `0057d82b` fall-through from the class-index path) with `0057d832 JZ` the sole bypass, gated on a null requested class that `005010e9` never passes. The second live alternative, that the check tests class identity, is excluded by `005727e6`'s own base-chain loop, which accepts any ancestor. Scope: a statement about `0057d47c`/`0057d6bc` and about descriptors as statically initialised in the image; a reader that does not go through `0057d47c`, or a descriptor built or patched at run time, is outside it, and neither is claimed against. `SAV-775` bounds what the corpus and the located writers actually produce at this one list. | ● active | [EXP-0320](../experiments/EXP-0320-item-effect-classes/) |
| SAV-775 | **No writer was located, and none was observed in a 98-file/5,134-item/234-member census of the preserved corpus, that places a class other than `Effect` into an item's own Effects list — and `SAV-774`'s reader-side check leaves only one class any writer could have placed there, `Effect`'s single subclass `Effect_DirectDamage`.** Two writers reach `Item::Serialize`'s list at `Item+0x20`. `ITEM-EFFGRAM-070`'s own `Effects=` grammar (`FUN_00502d82`, sole direct caller `005089f5`) appends each `FUN_00502eee` result; `00502d82`'s only `+0xc`-adjacent instruction is a stack-local parameter read (`MOV ECX,[EBP+0xc]` at `00502e5b`, an EBP-relative read of its own second stack argument — not an object write). A second writer reaches the same list, and it is not new: `FUN_0050ba63` is `SHOP-EFFALT-071`'s and `SHOP-MAGIC-007`'s own magic-shop effect-generator dispatch — confirmed the same routine here by re-deriving `SHOP-MAGIC-007`'s own "`2*ceiling-currentStoredPrice`" budget formula directly from its body (`0050bad1 SHL ECX,1` then `0050bad6 SUB ECX,[EDX+0x1c]` against the item's own `+0x1c`) — not a use-time writer, contrary to an earlier draft of this claim: neither this experiment nor `EXP-0226` places any of its callers at item use, only at the shop-stock route `SHOP-MAGIC-007`/`SHOP-EFFALT-071` already name. What those existing claims do not state is the field-level mechanism this experiment adds: `FUN_00508a60` has exactly four direct callers image-wide, a complete census, all four inside `FUN_0050ba63`'s own body; on one path `0050ba63` first calls `FUN_0050c055` (its sole reference to it, and `0050c055`'s own only direct caller image-wide) — which allocates 0x48 bytes, constructs through the default `Effect` constructor (`00501105`), hardcodes kind `0x29`/41, and sets the operand from a table (`0x59bc20`/`0x59bc28`). Kind 41 is confirmed as `castSpell` rather than assumed from list order: the 50-entry kind-name table `MAGIC-EFFECT-015` cites at `0x5c3190` stores one pointer per kind; index 41's own slot, at `0x5c3190+41*4=0x5c3234`, holds `0x005c5aec`, and the bytes at that address read `castspell\0` — exactly the node `SHOP-EFFCAST-065` already says this generator appends. `SHOP-EFFCAST-065` says only that a `castSpell` node is appended, not which field receives it or what happens on a repeat kind; this experiment supplies both: the target is the exact `Item+0x20` field `Item::Serialize` persists (`ITEM-SAVE-014`), and `FUN_00508a60` walks that list comparing each element's kind (`+0x3c`) against the new object's — a match updates that element's operand in place, no match appends the new object (`0051f830`/`0051f990`). Both writers' construction sites were swept for `[reg+0xc]` writes end to end (`00502eee`: 0x502eee-0x503660, zero writes beyond the default constructor's own zero; `0050c055`: 0x50c055-0x50c178, one `+0xc` access and it is a stack-local read; `00508a60`+`0050ba63`: zero `+0xc` accesses of any kind). The census (`evidence/member.tsv`, `evidence/summary.tsv`; 16 roots — `gameversions/en`, `gameversions/ru`, and 14 preserved save directories, the same population `EXP-0313` used) reads 98 file instances (one `Bsg&`-magic file excluded, an already-documented non-original artifact), of which 95 complete a full document walk — the remaining 3 are one already-published digest (`SAV-DOC-053`/`SAV-EFFECTGRAPH-366`'s sole failing stream, which desynchronises only inside a later `SpellTransport` serializer, after all `Player`/`Item` processing has completed, so their own item data is still captured) — spanning 5,134 items and 234 Effects-list member slots: every member's class is exactly `Effect`, and every one of the 234 carries Token `+0x0c`=0 (`SAV-776`). 57 of the 234 carry kind 41 (`0x29`), matching `FUN_0050c055`'s own hardcoded kind exactly — concrete evidence the shop generator's own merge routine fires in the preserved corpus, not only in static reachability. This does not close the question: a wider caller set reaching the shared list-append primitive only through intermediate thunk `0051f830` (11 direct callers total; 3 were disassembled and build unrelated CString-based text, not an Effects list; the rest, including a chain 13 callers deep through `FUN_004f36f7`, were not individually checked) was not exhaustively traced, so a third writer elsewhere in the image is not excluded. `SAV-774` bounds the other side of the question, and it bounds it tightly: the reader cannot accept an arbitrary class here at all. `SAV-CLASSSER-177`'s witnessed `SpellTransport` → `PointEffect` → `Effect_DirectDamage` graph in `game0018.sav` places an `Effect_DirectDamage` in a slot whose reader requests `Effect` (`SAV-CLASSSER-174`, the identical `0x5c3160` descriptor `005010e9` pushes for Item's own list), and that reference is accepted **because it passes** the derivation check at `0057d839`: `Effect_DirectDamage`'s descriptor at `0x5c3178` carries base pointer `0x5c3160` at `0x5c3188`. What that graph witnesses is therefore polymorphism through a checked subclass, not an unchecked substitution. Any unlocated writer into an item's own list is bound by the same check, so the widest class set it could produce is `{Effect, Effect_DirectDamage}` — and `Effect_DirectDamage` is the class `MAGIC-DMG-005`'s live damage-spell path constructs, which `SAV-776` places outside both located item-list writers' call graphs. | **Medium.** The population is exact and the two located writers' own construction sites are read end to end, but the negative — that no writer anywhere in the image places a non-`Effect` class into this one list — rests on a caller trace this experiment did not carry to exhaustion (the 11-caller thunk `0051f830`, the 13-deep chain through `FUN_004f36f7`), and corpus agreement over 234 members does not by itself exclude an unobserved writer. `SAV-774`'s reader-side check narrows what such a writer could produce to one alternative class, `Effect_DirectDamage`; it does not exclude a writer producing that one, so Medium and not High. | ● active | [EXP-0320](../experiments/EXP-0320-item-effect-classes/) |
| SAV-776 | **An item's own `Effect` record cannot carry a nonzero Token `+0x0c` from either located writer: both of them — item generation's own grammar and the magic-shop effect generator's merge step (`SAV-775`) — construct exclusively through the default constructor that zeroes it and never write it again, and the routines that do stamp a nonzero identity there belong to a call graph neither reaches.** `FUN_00502eee` — the shared "kind=value" builder `ITEM-EFFGRAM-070`'s grammar calls directly and the spell-arm template step `FUN_00501156` calls indirectly — allocates through the default constructor `FUN_00501105` (which zeroes `+0x3c`/`+0x3d`/`+0x40`/`+0x0c`/`+0x44`, `MAGIC-EFFECT-015`) and, across its full body (`0x502eee`-`0x503666`, one past the terminating `RET` at `0x503665` and exactly at the next prologue, verified by a register-relative-write sweep for `+0xc`/`+0x3c`/`+0x3d`/`+0x40`), writes only `+0x3c` (one site), `+0x3d` (two sites) and `+0x40` (five sites, matching the timed/untimed width split `MAGIC-EFFMODE-009` documents) — never `+0x0c`. `FUN_00502eee` has exactly two direct callers image-wide: `00501156` (`0x501190`) and `00502d82` (`0x502e46`, `ITEM-EFFGRAM-070`'s own comma-splitter, sole caller `005089f5`, item generation's entry point). `SAV-775`'s second writer, `FUN_0050c055`, constructs through the identical default constructor and likewise never writes `+0x0c` (swept end to end). In contrast, `MAGIC-DMG-005` already publishes that `FUN_004feadb` "sets `+0x0c` = the spell id" for a live `Effect_DirectDamage`, and `MAGIC-ATTACH-016` that "every arm stamps the spell id" for `FUN_004ff57d`'s eleven arms; this experiment locates the instructions those two claims describe only structurally — eleven direct `MOV [reg+0xc],reg8` sites inside `004ff57d` (e.g. `004ff957`, confirmed by disassembly to copy a byte from the enclosing `Spell` record's own `+0x8` into the new `Effect`'s `+0xc`, immediately after a `00501156` call that had just zeroed it) and three more inside `004feadb` (`004fedaa`/`004fef8e`/`004ff03d`) plus two inside adjacent `FUN_004ff254` (one a zeroing `AND`) — none of which item generation's own call graph reaches: `004ff57d`'s sole caller is `004fd09c` (itself called only from `004fcd16`); `004feadb`'s three callers are `004f36f7`, `004fe92e` and `004feaa2`; none of these, nor their own traced callers, is `005089f5` or any function this experiment found reachable from it. The shop generator's own writer is equally disjoint: `0050c055`'s sole caller and `00508a60`'s four callers are all `0050ba63` itself, none of the five addresses above. A 98-file/5,134-item/234-member corpus census (`SAV-775`) independently confirms Token `+0x0c`=0 in all 234 item-owned `Effect` records, widening `ITEM-EFFSAVE-077`'s own eight-witness sample by close to thirty times with no exception. | **High.** The construction-site sweep is exhaustive for both located writers (every register-relative write to the field is enumerated, not sampled), and `00502eee`'s exactly-two-caller census closes the shared-builder path completely; the live alternative the brief's Q2 poses — a nonzero `+0x0c` on an item's own `Effect` that simply has not been observed yet — is excluded structurally, not only by corpus absence, because the sole writers never contain an instruction capable of producing it. This does not exclude a writer entirely outside the traced call graph (the same bounded thunk-caller set `SAV-775` names); it is a claim about the writers this experiment located and followed to their own callers, not an image-wide instruction census — which is why the headline names that bound. A second model was checked and excluded at instruction level rather than argued away: a `REP MOVSB`/`MOVSW`/`MOVSD` block copy would overwrite `+0x0c` without any operand encoding the offset a displacement sweep looks for, so all four bodies were disassembled end to end and filtered for the string-move mnemonics; across the four the only hits are five `MOVSX` sign-extending loads inside `0050ba63` (`0050ba9a`, `0050bb41`, `0050bba3`, `0050bc21`, `0050bcee`), and no range reports a decode stop. Every function bound above is taken from disassembly through the terminating `RET` and the following prologue, not from `cursorsurf -fnof`, whose "next start" is wrong for `00502eee` (`00502ef1`) and `0050c055` (`0050c058`) and which reports three false interior starts (`00502ff3`, `0050348c`, `0050c0bc`) from `E8` bytes that are not instruction boundaries: inside a `MOV [EBP-0x18],0x0` encoding at `00503487` and `0050c0b7`, and straddling a `MOV [EBP-0x18],ECX` and the `JMP` after it at `004f9e05`. | ● active | [EXP-0320](../experiments/EXP-0320-item-effect-classes/) |

| SAV-790 | **The 400-byte trailer block's transfer is one non-virtual call site serving both directions, and the direction map is named: `0057b908` reads, `0057ba16` writes.** `-xcallers 53e800` returns exactly one direct E8/E9 site image-wide, `004d15ed` in `004d11d0`; the store arm reaches that same instruction by tail-jumping into the load arm's epilogue at `004d0efa e9 de 06 00 00 JMP 004d15dd`, so `SAV-TRAIL-026`'s two arms share one transfer. An absolute-dword sweep over every section returns one hit, `00558477`, and its own context bytes disqualify it: `68 44 82 5b 00 e8 53 00 00 00` is `PUSH 0x5b8244` followed by a `CALL rel32`, and the four "reference" bytes straddle the push immediate and the call opcode. No stored pointer to `0053e800` exists, so no vtable holds it; `0053e7d0` has the same shape, one caller `004ced4b` and zero dword references. Both archive primitives compute `[ESI+0x24]`, `[ESI+0x28]`, their difference and a min against the requested count, then call memcpy `00553f70`; the argument order separates them, since at `0057b933` the last value pushed is `[EBP+0x8]`, the caller's own buffer, making it memcpy's destination and the archive's buffer the source. `0053e800` complements `ar+0x14` and tests bit 0, so MFC's set loading bit reaches `0057b908`; eighteen executed vectors over six mode values agree and give the transfer count as `0x190` at every one. The block is transferred unconditionally on load: `004d15c7 CMP [EBP-0x10],0xbadface1` / `004d15ce JNZ 004d15dd` skips only `SAV-648`'s re-read of `[0x609b0c]`, and a stream whose marker does not match still has 400 bytes consumed at that position. | **High** — the two censuses are complete over their instrument's form (direct E8/E9 in `.text`; absolute dwords in every section), and they exclude the two live alternatives by construction: a direction-specific second transfer site would appear as a second E8/E9 hit, and a virtual dispatch would require a stored pointer, of which the one candidate is byte-for-byte inside a `CALL rel32` displacement. A call target computed into a register through arithmetic that never forms the literal would not be found; none is asserted. The unconditional load transfer is read from the branch target, not executed against a marker-less stream. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0321](../experiments/EXP-0321-sav-trailer-unit-fields/), `evidence/q1-serializer-census.txt`, `evidence/q1-block-sites.txt`, `evidence/closure/vectors.tsv` |

| SAV-793 | **The carried-load arithmetic is 16-bit and its 64 000 threshold compare is signed, and the encodings of its consumer imply that a load large enough to wrap reads negative and cancels the speed penalty rather than deepening it.** `004f81d0 CMP [EAX+0x20],0xfa00` is followed by `JGE`, not `JAE`: the container's weight sum is compared as a signed dword. `004f81f1 ADD DX,AX` is a 16-bit add and wraps at 0x10000, and only the low word of the 32-bit halved sum participates. The consumer reads the field back with `MOVSX` at `004f820f` and branches with `JL` at `004f8222`, so a stored value at or above 0x8000 is negative and the `load >= capacity` test fails. No preserved record reaches the wrap: the largest `+0x90` in the corpus is 457. | **High** for the widths, the signedness and the boundary — 110 executed vectors over own weights `{0, 1, 30, 0x7fff, 0xffff}` and sums `{-2, 0, 1, 2, 3, 224, 63998, 63999, 64000, 64001, 0x7fffffff}` with and without a container, each predicting a different stored value under a 32-bit accumulate or an unsigned compare. **Medium** for the behavioural consequence: `MOVSX` and `JL` are read from their encodings and the wrap is executed, but no vector runs the penalty branch itself and no preserved save reaches the wrapping range, so the claim that the penalty cancels rests on the two encodings and not on an observation of the game. | ✔ promoted | [EXP-0321](../experiments/EXP-0321-sav-trailer-unit-fields/), `evidence/closure/vectors.tsv`, `evidence/q2-derive-and-load-arm.txt` |
